\left\langle f,\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}g^{(n)}(\beta)e^{in\phi}\right\rangle=\left\langle\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}f^{(n)}(\beta)e^{in\phi},\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}g^{(n)}(\beta)e^{in\phi}\right\rangle=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\overline{f^{(n)}(\beta)}g^{(n)}(\beta)\,d\beta.

Note that
f
satisfies polynomially uniformly bounded condition (
3.6
) as

\displaystyle\left|\int_{0}^{\infty}f^{(n)}(\beta)g^{(n)}(\beta)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}\left\langle n\right\rangle^{s}\left|f^{(n)}(\beta)\right|\left\langle n\right\rangle^{-s}\left|g^{(n)}(\beta)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\underbrace{\left(\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\left\langle n\right\rangle^{s}\left\|f^{(n)}(\beta)\right\|_{X^{(n)}}\right)}_{\text{independent of }n}\left\langle n\right\rangle^{-s}\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(\frac{\left\langle\beta\right\rangle^{2}}{\beta}\right)^{-2}\underbrace{\left(\frac{\left\langle\beta\right\rangle^{2}}{\beta}\right)^{2}\left|g^{(n)}(\beta)\right|}_{\leq\left\|g^{(n)}\right\|_{\mathcal{S},2}}\,d\beta

\displaystyle\leq\underbrace{\Bigg(\left(\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\left\langle n\right\rangle^{s}\left\|f^{(n)}(\beta)\right\|_{X^{(n)}}\right)\underbrace{\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(\frac{\left\langle\beta\right\rangle^{2}}{\beta}\right)^{-2}\,d\beta}_{<\infty}\Bigg)}_{\text{constant independent of }n}\left\langle n\right\rangle^{-s}\left\|g^{(n)}\right\|_{\mathcal{S},2}.

Also, considering the definition of derivatives on tempered distribution, we have

\partial_{\beta}\left(\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}f^{(n)}(\beta)e^{in\phi}\right)=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}f^{(n)}_{\beta}(\beta)e^{in\phi},\qquad\partial_{\phi}\left(\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}f^{(n)}(\beta)e^{in\phi}\right)=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}inf^{(n)}(\beta)e^{in\phi}

in the sense of tempered distribution, and if regularity of
\left\{f^{(n)}(\beta)\right\}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}
is good enough, this will be true as a function. In the view of proposition
3.13
,
\partial_{\beta}
is induced by
\left\{\partial_{\beta}\right\}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}
and
\partial_{\phi}
is induced by multiplication operator
\left\{M_{in}\right\}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}
.

From now on, we omit ‘
M
’ in multiplication operator for simplicity, so we use
\left\{in\right\}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}
instead of
\left\{M_{in}\right\}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}
. Next, we investigate multiplication property of
\mathcal{A}^{s}
-space.

Proposition 3.15
.

Let
\left\{X^{(n)}\right\},\left\{Y^{(n)}\right\},\left\{Z^{(n)}\right\}
be sequences of Banach space which is
\left\langle n\right\rangle^{q}
-uniformly embedded in
X,Y,Z\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\overline{\mathbb{R}}_{+})
respectively for some
q\in\mathbb{R}
. Also, suppose
X,Y,Z
consist of functions on
\overline{\mathbb{R}}_{+}
(cf. Remark
3.6
) and

X^{(i)}\cdot Y^{(j)}\overset{\sim}{\hookrightarrow}Z^{(i+j)},

i.e., there exists
M>0
such that for all
f^{(i)}\in X^{(i)}
and
g^{(j)}\in Y^{(j)}
, we have
f^{(i)}g^{(j)}\in Z^{(i+j)}
with
\left\|f^{(i)}g^{(j)}\right\|_{Z^{(i+j)}}\leq M\left\|f^{(i)}\right\|_{X^{(i)}}\left\|g^{(j)}\right\|_{Y^{(j)}}
for all
i,j\in\mathbb{Z}
. Then, for all
s\geq 0
,

(1)

For
f\in\mathcal{A}^{s}\left(X^{(n)}\right),g\in\mathcal{A}^{s}\left(Y^{(n)}\right)
,
fg\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\left(\overline{\mathbb{R}}_{+}\times\mathbb{T}\right)
defined by

\left(fg\right)^{(n)}\coloneqq\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}f^{(n-k)}g^{(k)}

(3.8)

belongs to
\mathcal{A}^{s}\left(Z^{(n)}\right)
with norm bound

\left\|fg\right\|_{\mathcal{A}^{s}\left(Z^{(n)}\right)}\leq\sqrt{2}M\left\|f\right\|_{\mathcal{A}^{s}\left(X^{(n)}\right)}\left\|g\right\|_{\mathcal{A}^{s}\left(Y^{(n)}\right)}.

(2)

For
f\in\mathcal{A}^{s}\left(X^{(n)}\right),g\in\mathcal{A}^{-s}\left(Y^{(n)}\right)
,
fg\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\left(\overline{\mathbb{R}}_{+}\times\mathbb{T}\right)
defined by

\left(fg\right)^{(n)}\coloneqq\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}f^{(n-k)}g^{(k)}

(3.9)

belongs to
\mathcal{A}^{-s}\left(Z^{(n)}\right)
with norm bound

\left\|fg\right\|_{\mathcal{A}^{-s}\left(Z^{(n)}\right)}\leq\sqrt{2}M\left\|f\right\|_{\mathcal{A}^{s}\left(X^{(n)}\right)}\left\|g\right\|_{\mathcal{A}^{-s}\left(Y^{(n)}\right)}.

Proof.

We first prove (1). For
f\in\mathcal{A}^{s}\left(X^{(n)}\right)
and
g\in\mathcal{A}^{s}\left(Y^{(n)}\right)
,

\displaystyle\left\|fg\right\|_{\mathcal{A}^{s}(Z^{(n)})}

\displaystyle=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\left\langle n\right\rangle^{s}\left\|(fg)^{(n)}\right\|_{Z^{(n)}}

\displaystyle{\leq}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\left\langle n\right\rangle^{s}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\left\|f^{(n-k)}g^{(k)}\right\|_{Z^{(n)}}

\displaystyle{\leq}M\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\left\langle n\right\rangle^{s}\left\|f^{(n-k)}\right\|_{X^{(n-k)}}\left\|g^{(k)}\right\|_{Y^{(k)}}

\displaystyle{\leq}\sqrt{2}M\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\left\langle n-k\right\rangle^{s}\left\|f^{(n-k)}\right\|_{X^{(n-k)}}\left\langle k\right\rangle^{s}\left\|g^{(k)}\right\|_{Y^{(k)}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{2}M\left\|f\right\|_{\mathcal{A}^{s}\left(X^{(n)}\right)}\left\|g\right\|_{\mathcal{A}^{s}\left(Y^{(n)}\right)}.

Since
\left\|f\right\|_{\mathcal{A}^{s}\left(X^{(n)}\right)}<\infty
and
\left\|g\right\|_{\mathcal{A}^{s}\left(Y^{(n)}\right)}<\infty
, we derive
fg
defined by (
3.8
) is well-defined tempered distribution as infinite sum converges absolutely to an element of
Z^{(n)}
.

Proof of (2) is similar; just use
\left\langle n\right\rangle^{-1}\leq\sqrt{2}\left\langle n-k\right\rangle\left\langle k\right\rangle^{-1}
instead of
\left\langle n\right\rangle\leq\sqrt{2}\left\langle n-k\right\rangle\left\langle k\right\rangle
.
∎

3.2.
Suited function space

In this subsection, we introduce various Banach spaces which will provide the answer for
Question 1
. At the first glance, these spaces might look artificial, as we will not use familiar Banach spaces such as Lebesgue space or Sobolev space. However, the reasons why we have to choose these unintuitive space become clear.

Suppose
X
and
Y
are Banach spaces that is included in some vector space
V
. Then,
X\cap Y
is also a Banach space with its norm

\left\|a\right\|_{X\cap Y}\coloneqq\textnormal{max}\left\{\left\|a\right\|_{X},\left\|a\right\|_{Y}\right\}.

Also, if
X\cap Y=\emptyset
,
X\oplus Y
is a Banach space with its norm

\left\|x+y\right\|_{X\oplus Y}\coloneqq\left\|x\right\|_{X}+\left\|y\right\|_{Y}.

Even though
X
and
Y
are not disjoint,
X+Y
is still a Banach space with its norm

\left\|a\right\|_{X+Y}=\inf\left\{\left\|x\right\|_{X}+\left\|y\right\|_{Y}\,|\,a=x+y,x\in X,y\in Y\right\}.

The function
\xi_{\infty}:[0,\infty)\rightarrow[0,1]
is a smooth function which is 0 near the origin and 1 near the infinity. More specific conditions imposed on
\xi_{\infty}
will be clarified later. Also, we define
\xi_{0}
to be
1-\xi_{\infty}
. Now we are ready to introduce important Banach spaces which will be main characters of this paper;

\displaystyle C_{b}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\left\{f:[0,\infty)\rightarrow\mathbb{C}\,\Big|\,f\text{ is continuous with }\left\|f\right\|_{C_{b}}\coloneqq\sup_{\beta\in[0,\infty)}\left|f(\beta)\right|<\infty\right\},

(3.10)

\displaystyle C_{b}^{\delta}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\beta^{\delta}C_{b}\cap\beta^{-\delta}C_{b}=\left\{f\in C_{b}\,\Big|\,\left\|f\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}\coloneqq\textnormal{max}\left\{\left\|\beta^{\delta}f\right\|_{C_{b}},\left\|\beta^{-\delta}f\right\|_{C_{b}}\right\}<\infty\right\},

(3.11)

\displaystyle W_{-}^{(n)}

\displaystyle\coloneqq C_{b}^{\delta}\oplus\delta_{0n}\mathbb{C}\xi_{0}\coloneqq\begin{cases}C_{b}^{\delta}\oplus\mathbb{C}\xi_{0}&(n=0)\\
C_{b}^{\delta}&(n\neq 0)\end{cases},

(3.12)

\displaystyle W_{0}^{(n)}

\displaystyle\coloneqq C_{b}^{\delta}\oplus\mathbb{C}\xi_{0}\oplus\delta_{0n}\mathbb{C},

(3.13)

\displaystyle W_{+}^{(n)}

\displaystyle\coloneqq C_{b}^{\delta}\oplus\mathbb{C}\xi_{0}\oplus\mathbb{C}\xi_{\infty},

(3.14)

\displaystyle\Box_{,N}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\begin{cases}\Box&(n=N\mathbb{Z})\\
0&(n\neq N\mathbb{Z})\end{cases},\qquad\text{where }\Box=C_{b},C_{b}^{\delta},W^{(n)}_{-},W^{(n)}_{0},W^{(n)}_{+}.

(3.15)

Note that

C_{b}^{\delta}\overset{\sim}{\hookrightarrow}W^{(n)}_{-}\overset{\sim}{\hookrightarrow}W^{(n)}_{0}\overset{\sim}{\hookrightarrow}W^{(n)}_{+}\overset{\sim}{\hookrightarrow}C_{b}\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}_{+}),

(3.16)

where
X^{(n)}\overset{\sim}{\hookrightarrow}Y^{(n)}
means
X^{(n)}\subset Y^{(n)}
and
id_{[x^{(n)},Y^{(n)}]}
is uniformly bounded for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}
. Above spaces have multiplication property also.

Proposition 3.16
.

For all
N\geq 0
,

\displaystyle W^{(i)}_{-,N}\cdot W^{(j)}_{+,N}

\displaystyle\overset{\sim}{\hookrightarrow}W^{(i+j)}_{-,N},

(3.17)

\displaystyle W^{(i)}_{0,N}\cdot W^{(j)}_{0,N}

\displaystyle\overset{\sim}{\hookrightarrow}W^{(i+j)}_{0,N},

(3.18)

\displaystyle W^{(i)}_{+,N}\cdot W^{(j)}_{+,N}

\displaystyle\overset{\sim}{\hookrightarrow}W^{(i+j)}_{+,N}.

(3.19)

Proof.

Write an element of
W^{(i)}_{\triangle,N}
, where
\triangle
could be either
-,0,+
, explicitly and use elementary inequalities

\left\|fg\right\|_{C_{b}}\leq\left\|f\right\|_{C_{b}}\left\|g\right\|_{C_{b}},

\displaystyle\left\|f\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}

\displaystyle=\textnormal{max}\left\{\left\|\beta^{\delta}f\right\|_{C_{b}},\left\|\beta^{-\delta}f\right\|_{C_{b}}\right\}

\displaystyle=\sup_{\beta\in[0,\infty)}\underbrace{\textnormal{max}\left\{\beta^{\delta},\beta^{-\delta}\right\}}_{\geq 1}\left|f(\beta)\right|

\displaystyle\geq\left\|f\right\|_{C_{b}}.

∎

Since
W_{\triangle,N}^{(n)}
is uniformly embedded in
C_{b}
, we can define the following space.

Definition 3.17
.

\displaystyle W_{-,N}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\mathcal{A}^{0.5}\left(W^{(n)}_{-,N}\right),

\displaystyle W_{0,N}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\mathcal{A}^{0.5}\left(W^{(n)}_{0,N}\right),

\displaystyle W_{+,N}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\mathcal{A}^{0.5}\left(W^{(n)}_{+,N}\right).

For three Banach space
X,Y
and
Z
, we write
X\cdot Y\hookrightarrow Z
if there exists
M>0
such that for all
f\in X,g\in Y
, we have
fg\in Z
with
\left\|fg\right\|_{Z}\leq M\left\|f\right\|_{X}\left\|g\right\|_{Y}
holds. Using this notation, we can prove the following
algebra
property of
W_{\triangle,N}
.

Proposition 3.18
.

For all
N\geq 0
,

\displaystyle W_{-,N}\cdot W_{+,N}\hookrightarrow W_{-,N},

(3.20)

\displaystyle W_{0,N}\cdot W_{0,N}\hookrightarrow W_{0,N},

(3.21)

\displaystyle W_{+,N}\cdot W_{+,N}\hookrightarrow W_{+,N}.

(3.22)

Proof.

Proposition
3.15
and Proposition
3.16
.
∎

Now, we are ready to construct Banach space to where we apply implicit function theorem to
\overline{L}:X\times Y\rightarrow Z
defined as in (
3.1
). We have to choose
X,Y,Z
so that
\overline{L}
is
C^{1}
and
\frac{\partial\overline{L}}{\partial\overline{\psi}}\left(\overline{\psi}_{0},\Omega_{0}\right)
is an isomorphism. As isomorphism condition is more strict than to just require its Fréchet derivative to be bounded, we construct
X,Y,Z
so that we easily guarantee
\frac{\partial\overline{L}}{\partial\psi}(\overline{\psi}_{0},\Omega_{0})
is an isomorphism.
To utilize Proposition
3.13
, we first decompose partial Fréchet derivative (
3.3
) into each Fourier mode. From now on, if a map
T:\mathcal{A}^{s}\left(X^{(n)}\right)\rightarrow\mathcal{A}^{s}\left(Y^{(n)}\right)
is induced by sequence of uniformly bounded map
G^{(n)}
as in proposition
3.13
, we use notation
T^{(n)}
to represent
G^{(n)}
. Then, differential operators on
\mathcal{A}^{s}\left(X^{(n)}\right)
were decomposed into

\displaystyle\partial_{\beta}^{(n)}

\displaystyle=\partial_{\beta},

(3.23)

\displaystyle\partial_{\phi}^{(n)}

\displaystyle=in,

(3.24)

\displaystyle\partial_{\varphi}^{(n)}

\displaystyle=in-\partial_{\beta},

(3.25)

\displaystyle\overline{\partial}_{\beta}^{(n)}

\displaystyle{=}\left(\beta\partial_{\beta}+(1-2\mu)\right)^{(n)}\underset{\eqref{diff beta n}}{=}\beta\partial_{\beta}+(1-2\mu),

(3.26)

\displaystyle\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}^{(n)}

\displaystyle{=}\left(\beta\partial_{\varphi}+(2\mu-1)\right)^{(n)}\underset{\eqref{diff varphi n}}{=}\beta in-\beta\partial_{\beta}+(2\mu-1),

(3.27)

\displaystyle\left(\left(\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}+1\right)\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\right)^{(n)}

\displaystyle{=}\left((\beta\partial_{\varphi}+2\mu)(\beta\partial_{\beta}+(1-2\mu)\right)^{(n)}

\displaystyle=\left(\beta\partial_{\varphi}\beta\partial_{\beta}+(1-2\mu)\beta\partial_{\varphi}+2\mu\beta\partial_{\beta}+2\mu(1-2\mu)\right)^{(n)}

\displaystyle=(\beta in-\beta\partial_{\beta})\beta\partial_{\beta}+(1-2\mu)(\beta in-\beta\partial_{\beta})+2\mu\beta\partial_{\beta}+2\mu(1-2\mu),

(3.28)

\displaystyle\left(\partial_{\phi}\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\right)^{(n)}

\displaystyle{=}\left(\partial_{\phi}(\beta\partial_{\beta}+1-2\mu)\right)^{(n)}\underset{\eqref{diff phi n}}{=}in\beta\partial_{\beta}+in(1-2\mu).

(3.29)

Then, we can decompose (
3.3
) to each Fourier mode using (
3.26
) and (
3.27
);

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\partial\overline{L}}{\partial\overline{\psi}}\left(\overline{\psi}_{0},\Omega_{0}\right)\right)^{(n)}

\displaystyle{=}\frac{1}{2\mu^{2}}\left((\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}+\mu^{2}\partial_{\phi}\partial_{\phi})(\overline{\partial}_{\beta}+2\mu)+(2\mu-1)(\overline{\partial}_{\beta}+\overline{\partial}_{\varphi})\right)^{(n)}

\displaystyle{=}\frac{1}{2\mu^{2}}\left(\left(\left(\beta\left(in-\partial_{\beta}\right)+\left(2\mu-1\right)\right)^{2}-\mu^{2}n^{2}\right)\left(\beta\partial_{\beta}+1\right)+\left(2\mu-1\right)in\beta\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\mu^{2}}(\left(\beta\left(\partial_{\beta}-in\right)-\left(\left(2+n\right)\mu-1\right)\right)\left(\beta\left(\partial_{\beta}-in\right)-\left(\left(2-n\right)\mu-1\right)\right)\left(\beta\partial_{\beta}+1\right)

\displaystyle+\left(2\mu-1\right)in\beta)

Observation of (
3.26
), (
3.27
), (
3.28
), (
3.29
) and
\left(\frac{\partial\overline{L}}{\partial\overline{\psi}}\left(\overline{\psi}_{0},\Omega_{0}\right)\right)^{(n)}
reveals similar expression of the following form.

Definition 3.19
.

For
n\in\mathbb{Z},s\in\mathbb{R}
, the operator
D^{\left(n,s\right)}
is defined by

D^{\left(n,s\right)}\coloneqq\beta\left(\partial_{\beta}-in\right)-s.

(3.30)

To simplify expression more, we use the following notation.

\displaystyle P^{(n)}

\displaystyle\coloneqq D^{\left(n,0\right)}=\beta\left(\partial_{\beta}-in\right),

(3.31)

\displaystyle P_{+}^{(n)}

\displaystyle\coloneqq D^{\left(n,(2+n)\mu-1\right)}=\beta\left(\partial_{\beta}-in\right)-\left(\left(2+n\right)\mu-1\right),

(3.32)

\displaystyle P_{-}^{(n)}

\displaystyle\coloneqq D^{\left(n,(2-n)\mu-1\right)}=\beta\left(\partial_{\beta}-in\right)-\left(\left(2-n\right)\mu-1\right),

(3.33)

\displaystyle Q

\displaystyle\coloneqq D^{\left(0,0\right)}=\beta\partial_{\beta}.

(3.34)

Then, above operators are represented simply by

\displaystyle\left(\beta\partial_{\beta}\right)^{(n)}

\displaystyle=Q,

(3.35)

\displaystyle\left(\beta\partial_{\varphi}\right)^{(n)}

\displaystyle=-P^{(n)},

(3.36)

\displaystyle\left(\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\right)^{(n)}

\displaystyle=Q+(1-2\mu)id,

(3.37)

\displaystyle\left(\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}\right)^{(n)}

\displaystyle=-P^{(n)}+(2\mu-1)id,

(3.38)

\displaystyle\left(\partial_{\phi}\right)^{(n)}

\displaystyle=in\cdot id,

(3.39)

\displaystyle\left(\left(\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}+1\right)\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\right)^{(n)}

\displaystyle=-P^{(n)}Q+(2\mu-1)P^{(n)}+2\mu Q+2\mu(1-2\mu)id,

(3.40)

\displaystyle\left(\partial_{\phi}\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\right)^{(n)}

\displaystyle=inQ+in(1-2\mu)id,

(3.41)

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\partial\overline{L}}{\partial\overline{\psi}}\left(\overline{\psi}_{0},\Omega_{0}\right)\right)^{(n)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\mu^{2}}\left(P_{+}^{(n)}P_{-}^{(n)}\left(Q+1\right)+\left(2\mu-1\right)\left(Q-P^{(n)}\right)\right).

(3.42)

To apply implicit function theorem, we have to show that (
3.42
) is an isomorphism between some Banach space, but observe that (
3.42
) consists of sum of two linear map. To prove sum of linear map is isomorphism, we will show that
P_{+}^{(n)}P_{-}^{(n)}(Q+1)
is isomorphism and operator norm of
(2\mu-1)\left(Q-P^{(n)}\right)
could be small if we choose
N
large in (
3.15
). However, how can we prove
P_{+}^{(n)}P_{-}^{(n)}(Q+1)
is an isomorphism? Actually, it is more precise to say that we do not prove it, but construct space so that
P_{+}^{(n)}P_{-}^{(n)}(Q+1)
have no choice but to be an isometric isomorphism. The key is Lemma
3.10
, but to apply it, we have to verify that the operator is injective. Therefore, we need to investigate the property of operator
D^{\left(n,s\right)}
.

3.3.
Properties of operator
D^{(n,s)}

In this subsection, we investigate the properties of operator

D^{\left(n,s\right)}=\beta\left(\partial_{\beta}\ -in\right)-s.

First, to utilize Lemma
3.10
, we check the injectivity of
D^{\left(n,s\right)}
.

Proposition 3.20
.

For
s\neq 0
,
D^{\left(n,s\right)}
is injective on
C_{b}
.
12
12

12

Note that derivative is defined in distribution sense.

Proof.

An ODE

D^{\left(n,s\right)}f=\beta\partial_{\beta}f-\beta inf-sf=0

has a general solution

f(\beta)=C\beta^{s}e^{in\beta}

for
C\in\mathbb{R}
. Due to
\beta^{s}
-term for
s\neq 0
,
f
belongs to
C_{b}
if and only if
c=0
.
∎

Since
D^{\left(n,s\right)}
is injective whenever
s\neq 0
in
C_{b}
with
W_{\triangle}^{(n)}\overset{\sim}{\hookrightarrow}C_{b}
for
\triangle=+,0,-
, one can think of the following trivial strategy to make
P_{+}^{(n)}P_{-}^{(n)}(Q+1)
isometric isomorphism; Using Lemma
3.10
, set the domain of
P_{+}^{(n)}P_{-}^{(n)}(Q+1)
to one of
W^{(n)}_{\triangle}
, say
W^{(n)}_{0}
, and set the codomain of
P_{+}^{(n)}P_{-}^{(n)}(Q+1)
to
P_{+}^{(n)}P_{-}^{(n)}(Q+1)W^{(n)}_{0}
. However, this attempt might fail, because we do not have any guarantee that
(Q+1)W^{(n)}_{0}
belongs to
C_{b}
. If we take differential operator on
C_{b}
, regularity of function is getting worse. Therefore, we have to set the domain of
P_{+}^{(n)}P_{-}^{(n)}(Q+1)
to sufficiently regular space so that a function can maintain its boundedness after passing through differential operator. To achieve this, we set the domain to inverse of differential operator so that differential operator could be cancelled. With this motivation in mind, we investigate the properties of
\left(D^{\left(n,s\right)}\right)^{-1}
.

Proposition 3.21
.

For
s\neq 0
, the range of
D^{\left(n,s\right)}:C_{b}\rightarrow D^{\left(n,s\right)}C_{b}
contains
C_{b}
so that
\left(D^{\left(n,s\right)}\right)^{-1}:C_{b}\rightarrow C_{b}
is well-defined with

\left\|\left(D^{\left(n,s\right)}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{C_{b}}\leq\frac{1}{\left|s\right|}.

(3.43)

Proof.

Solving the ODE

D^{\left(n,s\right)}u=\beta\partial_{\beta}u-\beta inu-su=f

for
f\in C_{b}
gives

\displaystyle\left(D^{\left(n,s\right)}\right)^{-1}f

\displaystyle=\beta^{s}e^{in\beta}\int_{C}^{\beta}=\frac{f(x)e^{-inx}}{x^{s+1}}\,dx

\displaystyle{=}\beta^{s}e^{in\beta}\int_{C\beta}^{1}=\frac{f(\beta y)e^{-in\beta y}}{\beta^{s+1}y^{s+1}}\beta\,dy

\displaystyle=e^{in\beta}\int_{\frac{C}{\beta}}^{1}\frac{f(\beta y)e^{-in\beta y}}{y^{s+1}}\,dy,

where
C\geq 0
is an integral constant. Since the domain of
D^{\left(n,s\right)}
is
C_{b}
, we are forced to choose

C=\begin{cases}\infty&(s>0)\\
0&(s<0)\end{cases}

to make integral converge. Then, we have

\left(D^{\left(n,s\right)}\right)^{-1}f=\begin{cases}e^{in\beta}\int_{\infty}^{1}\frac{f(\beta y)e^{-in\beta y}}{y^{s+1}}\,dy&(s>0)\\
e^{in\beta}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{f(\beta y)e^{-in\beta y}}{y^{s+1}}\,dy&(s>0)\end{cases}

(3.44)

with

\displaystyle\left\|\left(D^{\left(n,s\right)}\right)^{-1}f\right\|_{C_{b}}\leq\left\|f\right\|_{C_{b}}\int_{1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{y^{s+1}}\,dy=\frac{1}{s}\left\|f\right\|_{C_{b}}<\infty\qquad

\displaystyle(s>0),

\displaystyle\left\|\left(D^{\left(n,s\right)}\right)^{-1}f\right\|_{C_{b}}\leq\left\|f\right\|_{C_{b}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{y^{s+1}}\,dy=-\frac{1}{s}\left\|f\right\|_{C_{b}}<\infty\qquad

\displaystyle(s>0).

Hence,
\left(D^{\left(n,s\right)}\right)^{-1}f\in C_{b}
for all
f\in C_{b}
, which implies
C_{b}\subset D^{\left(n,s\right)}C_{b}
for
s\neq 0
.
∎

Using Proposition
3.21
, we define a following space which will be the space where
\overline{\psi}
and the codomain of
\overline{L}
lives.

Definition 3.22
.

\displaystyle X_{0}^{(n)}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}\left(P_{-}^{(n)}\right)^{-1}W_{0}^{(n)},

(3.45)

\displaystyle Z_{0}^{(n)}

\displaystyle\coloneqq P_{+}^{(n)}W^{(n)}_{0},

(3.46)

\displaystyle\Box_{,N}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\begin{cases}\Box&n=N\mathbb{Z}\\
0&n\neq N\mathbb{Z}\end{cases}\qquad\text{where}\qquad\Box=X_{0}^{(n)},Z_{0}^{(n)}

With this definition, the fact that
P^{(n)}_{-}(Q+1):X^{(n)}_{0,N}\rightarrow W^{(n)}_{0,N}
is isometric isomorphism is a direct corollary of Lemma
3.10
. Before we check the continuity of the other differential operators using Proposition
3.13
, we need to check

(Q+1)^{-1}\left(P^{(n)}\right)^{-1}W_{0}^{(n)}\overset{\sim}{\hookrightarrow}K\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\overline{\mathbb{R}}_{+})

(3.47)

for some Banach space
K
. Since

W^{(n)}_{0,N}=C_{b,N}^{\delta}\oplus\mathbb{C}\xi_{0,N}\oplus\delta_{0n}\mathbb{C}\hookrightarrow C_{b},

it suffices to check

\left\|\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{[C_{b},C_{b}]}\underset{\eqref{Q},\eqref{Pnm}}{=}\left\|\left(D^{\left(0,-1\right)}\right)^{-1}\left(D^{\left(n,(2-n)\mu-1\right)}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{[C_{b},C_{b}]}\underset{\eqref{D^{-1} C_b}}{=}\frac{1}{\left|(2-n)\mu-1\right|}

is uniformly bounded for all
n\in N\mathbb{Z}
. It seems trivial, but what if
(2-n)\mu-1
is zero for some
n\in N\mathbb{Z}
? To prevent this, we impose some condition on
\mu,N
; from now on, we assume

N\geq 2,\qquad\mu>\frac{2}{3}.

(3.48)

With this assumption,
\left|(2-n)\mu-1\right|
never touches zero and
\frac{1}{\left|(2-n)\mu-1\right|}
is uniformly bounded for
n\in N\mathbb{Z}
. This shows (
3.47
) for
K=C_{b}
.

Now we treat boundedness property of operator
\left(D^{\left(n,s\right)}\right)^{-1}
on several spaces we have defined.

Proposition 3.23
.

For
s\notin[-\delta,\delta]
, the range of
D^{\left(n,s\right)}:C_{b}^{\delta}\rightarrow D^{\left(n,s\right)}C_{b}^{\delta}
contains
C_{b}^{\delta}
so that
\left(D^{\left(n,s\right)}\right)^{-1}:C_{b}^{\delta}\rightarrow C_{b}^{\delta}
is well-defined with

\left\|\left(D^{\left(n,s\right)}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}\leq\frac{1}{\mathrm{dist}\left([-\delta,\delta],s\right)}.

(3.49)

Proof.

This is also elementary like Proposition
3.21
. Since
C_{b}^{\delta}\hookrightarrow C_{b}
,
D^{\left(n,s\right)}\big|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}{}
is injective and its inverse is well-defined by Proposition
3.20
. For
s>0
,

\displaystyle\left\|\left(D^{\left(n,s\right)}\right)^{-1}f\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}

\displaystyle{=}\underset{+,-}{\max}\left\{\left\|\beta^{\pm s}\int_{\infty}^{1}\frac{f(\beta t)e^{-in\beta t}}{t^{s+1}}\,dt\right\|_{C_{b}}\right\}

\displaystyle=\underset{+,-}{\max}\left\{\left\|\int_{\infty}^{1}\frac{\left(\beta t\right)^{\pm\delta}f(\beta t)e^{in\beta t}}{t^{s+1\pm\delta}}\,dt\right\|_{C_{b}}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|f\right\|_{C_{b}}\underset{+,-}{\max}\int_{1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{t^{s+1\pm\delta}}\,dt

\displaystyle\leq\left\|f\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}\int_{1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{t^{s+1-\delta}}\,dt

\displaystyle{\leq}\frac{1}{s-\delta}\left\|f\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}.

Similarly for
s<0
,

\displaystyle\left\|\left(D^{\left(n,s\right)}\right)^{-1}f\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}

\displaystyle{=}\underset{+,-}{\max}\left\{\left\|\beta^{\pm s}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{f(\beta t)e^{-in\beta t}}{t^{s+1}}\,dt\right\|_{C_{b}}\right\}

\displaystyle{\leq}\left\|f\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}\frac{1}{s+\delta}.

Combining these results, we have

\left\|\left(D^{\left(n,s\right)}\right)^{-1}f\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}\leq\begin{cases}\frac{1}{s-\delta}\left\|f\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}&(s>\delta)\\
\frac{1}{s+\delta}\left\|f\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}&(s<-\delta)\end{cases}=\frac{1}{\mathrm{dist}\left([-\delta,\delta],s\right)}\left\|f\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}

∎

In this paper, (
3.49
) will be the most frequently used inequality since
P_{+}^{(n)},P_{-}^{(n)},Q
are the special case of
D^{\left(n,s\right)}
, where
s=(2+n)\mu-1,(2-n)\mu-1,-1
respectively. Therefore, we need to calculate
\text{dist}([-\delta,\delta],(2\pm n)\mu-1)
and set
\delta
more concretely.

Proposition 3.24
.

Suppose
\mu>\frac{2}{3}
and set

\delta=\frac{1}{2}\min\{2\mu-1,1\}.

(3.50)

Then, for
\left|n\right|\geq N\geq 4
,

\frac{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}{\left\langle N\right\rangle}\left\langle n\right\rangle\leq\mathrm{dist}\left([-\delta,\delta],(2\pm n)\mu-1\right)\leq\frac{(N+2)\mu-\frac{3}{2}}{\left\langle N\right\rangle}\left\langle n\right\rangle.

(3.51)

Proof.

By symmetry, we assume
n
is positive. First, we estimate

\frac{\mathrm{dist}\left([-\delta,\delta],(2+n)\mu-1\right)}{\left\langle n\right\rangle}=\frac{(2+n)\mu-1-\delta}{\left\langle n\right\rangle}.

Consider
f:[4,\infty)\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
defined by

f(x)=\frac{(2+x)\mu-1-\delta}{\sqrt{x^{2}+1}}.

As

f^{\prime}(x)=\frac{\mu\sqrt{x^{2}+1}-\frac{\left((2+x)\mu-1-\delta\right)x}{\sqrt{x^{2}+1}}}{x^{2}+1}=\frac{\mu-(2\mu-1-\delta)x}{\left(x^{2}+1\right)^{\frac{3}{2}}},

we have

f^{\prime}(4)=\frac{4}{17\sqrt{17}}\left(\delta-\left(\frac{7}{4}\mu-1\right)\right).

Using elementary inequality

\delta=\frac{1}{2}\min\{2\mu-1,1\}\leq\frac{7}{4}\mu-1

on
\mu>\frac{2}{3}
, we have
f^{\prime}(x)\leq 0
for
x\geq 4
. Since

f(N)=\frac{(2+N)\mu-1-\delta}{\left\langle N\right\rangle}\underset{\text{(}\delta\leq\frac{1}{2}\text{)}}{\geq}\frac{(2+N)\mu-\frac{3}{2}}{\left\langle N\right\rangle}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\lim_{x\rightarrow\infty}f(x)=\mu,

we have

\mu\leq\frac{\mathrm{dist}\left([-\delta,\delta],(2+n)\mu-1\right)}{\left\langle n\right\rangle}\leq\frac{(2+N)\mu-\frac{3}{2}}{\left\langle N\right\rangle}

for
n\geq N\geq 4
.

Now, we estimate

\frac{\mathrm{dist}\left([-\delta,\delta],(2-n)\mu-1\right)}{\left\langle n\right\rangle}=\frac{(n-2)\mu+1-\delta}{\left\langle n\right\rangle}.

Consider
g:[4,\infty)\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
defined by

g(x)=\frac{(x-2)\mu+1-\delta}{\sqrt{x^{2}+1}}.

As

g^{\prime}(x)=\frac{\mu\sqrt{x^{2}+1}-\left((x-2)\mu+1-\delta\right)\frac{x}{\sqrt{x^{2}+1}}}{x^{2}+1}=\frac{\mu+(2\mu-1+\delta)x}{\left(x^{2}+1\right)^{\frac{3}{2}}},

we know
g^{\prime}(x)\geq 0
for
x\geq 4
. Since

g(N)=\frac{(N-2)\mu+1-\delta}{\left\langle N\right\rangle}\underset{\text{(}\delta>\frac{1}{6}\text{)}}{\leq}\frac{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}{\left\langle N\right\rangle}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\lim_{x\rightarrow\infty}g(x)=\mu,

we have

\frac{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}{\left\langle N\right\rangle}\leq\frac{\mathrm{dist}\left([-\delta,\delta],(2-n)\mu-1\right)}{\left\langle n\right\rangle}\leq\mu.

Combining estimates for
f
and
g
, we get the result.
∎

From now on, we always assume
\mu,N,\delta
satisfy assumption in Proposition
3.24
.

Corollary 3.25
.

\displaystyle\left\|P_{\pm}^{(n)}\right\|_{[C_{b,N}^{\delta},C_{b,N}^{\delta}]}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\left\langle N\right\rangle}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\cdot\frac{1}{\left\langle n\right\rangle}.

(3.52)

\displaystyle\left\|(Q+1)\right\|_{[C_{b,N}^{\delta},C_{b,N}^{\delta}]}

\displaystyle\leq 2.

(3.53)

Proof.

Proposition
3.23
and
3.24
.
∎

Now, we calculate
\left(D^{\left(n,s\right)}\right)^{-1}f
when
f
belongs to
\mathbb{C}
, a space of the constant function defined on
[0,\infty)
. Observe in (
3.13
) that
W_{0}^{(n)}
contains
\mathbb{C}
only when
n=0
. The reason we included
\mathbb{C}
on zero frequency is that we should include trivial solution (
3.2
) to the domain of
\overline{L}
. Therefore, we show boundedness of
\left(D^{\left(n,s\right)}\right)^{-1}
on
\mathbb{C}
only for
n=0
.

Proposition 3.26
.

For
s\neq 0
, the range of
D^{\left(0,s\right)}:\mathbb{C}\rightarrow D^{\left(0,s\right)}\mathbb{C}
contains
\mathbb{C}
so that
\left(D^{\left(0,s\right)}\right)^{-1}:\mathbb{C}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
is well-defined with

\left\|\left(D^{\left(0,s\right)}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{\mathbb{C}}\leq\frac{1}{\left|s\right|}.

(3.54)

Proof.

Note that
\mathbb{C}\hookrightarrow C_{b}
so that
\left(D^{\left(0,s\right)}\right)^{-1}
is well-defined by Proposition
3.20
. As

D^{\left(0,s\right)}c=\left(\beta\partial_{\beta}-s\right)c=-sc

for
c\in\mathbb{C}
, we have

\left(D^{\left(0,s\right)}\right)^{-1}c=-\frac{1}{s}c.

∎

Next, we calculate
\left(D^{\left(n,s\right)}\right)^{-1}f
when
f
belongs to
\mathbb{C}\xi_{0}
.

Proposition 3.27
.

For
s\notin[-\delta,\delta]
,
\left(D^{\left(n,s\right)}\right)^{-1}:\mathbb{C}\xi_{0}\rightarrow C_{b}^{\delta}\oplus\mathbb{C}\xi_{0}
is continuous with exact norm bound

\left\|\left(D^{\left(n,s\right)}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{[\mathbb{C}\xi_{0},C_{b}^{\delta}\oplus\mathbb{C}\xi_{0}]}\leq\frac{1}{\left|s\right|}\left(\frac{\left\|\beta\partial_{\beta}\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}+\left|n\right|\left\|\beta\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}}{\mathrm{dist}\left([-\delta,\delta],s\right)}+1\right).

(3.55)

Proof.

As

D^{\left(n,s\right)}\xi_{0}=\underbrace{\beta\partial_{\beta}\xi_{0}}_{C_{b}^{\delta}}-\underbrace{in\beta\xi_{0}}_{C_{b}^{\delta}}-\underbrace{s\xi_{0}}_{\mathbb{C}\xi_{0}}

and
D^{\left(n,s\right)}
is injective on
C_{b}
, taking
\left(D^{\left(n,s\right)}\right)^{-1}
both side gives

\left(D^{\left(n,s\right)}\right)^{-1}\xi_{0}=\frac{1}{s}\left(\left(D^{\left(n,s\right)}\right)^{-1}(\beta\partial_{\beta}\xi_{0})-in\left(D^{\left(n,s\right)}\right)^{-1}(\beta\xi_{0})-\xi_{0}\right).

(3.56)

From this inequality, we have (
3.55
).
∎

Proposition
3.27
with Proposition
3.24
gives the following corollary.

Corollary 3.28
.

\displaystyle\left\|\left(P_{\pm}^{(n)}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{[\mathbb{C}\xi_{0,N},C_{b,N}^{\delta}\oplus\mathbb{C}\xi_{0,N}]}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\left\langle N\right\rangle}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\left(\frac{\left\|\beta\partial_{\beta}\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}+\left\langle N\right\rangle\left\|\beta\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}+1\right)\cdot\frac{1}{\left\langle n\right\rangle}.

(3.57)

\displaystyle\left\|\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}\right\|_{[\mathbb{C}\xi_{0,N},C_{b,N}^{\delta}\oplus\mathbb{C}\xi_{0,N}]}

\displaystyle\leq 2\left\|\beta\partial_{\beta}\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}+1.

(3.58)

3.4.
Continuity results for differential operator in function space

In this subsection, we deal with
C^{1}
continuity of
\overline{L}:X\times Y\rightarrow Z
, where the spaces
X,Y,Z
will be clarified. Before we do this, we show the following two lemmas related to commutativity of a differential operator with a multiplication operator. First, we treat the commutativity of
D^{\left(n,s\right)}
and multiplication operator
\beta^{\ell}
.

Lemma 3.29
.

For all
f\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\overline{\mathbb{R}}_{+})
and
n,s,\ell\in\mathbb{R}
,

D^{\left(n,s\right)}\left(\beta^{\ell}f\right)=\beta^{\ell}D^{\left(n,s-\ell\right)}f

Proof.

Considering the definition of tempered distribution, it suffices to check that

\left\langle D^{\left(n,s\right)}\left(\beta^{\ell}f\right),g\right\rangle=\left\langle\beta^{\ell}D^{\left(n,s-\ell\right)}f,g\right\rangle

for all
g\in\mathcal{S}(\overline{\mathbb{R}}_{+})
. This is just elementary calculus.
∎

Next, we deal with commutativity of
\left(D^{\left(n,s\right)}\right)^{-1}
and
\beta^{\ell}
. Unlike Lemma
3.29
, there is some remainder term.

Proposition 3.30
.

Suppose
f\in D^{\left(n,s-\ell\right)}C_{b}
,
\beta^{\ell}f\in D^{\left(n,s\right)}C_{b}
for some
n,s,\ell\in\mathbb{R}
, and
s,s-\ell\neq 0
. Then, there exists a constant
c\in\mathbb{C}
such that

\beta^{\ell}\left(D^{\left(n,s-\ell\right)}\right)^{-1}f=\left(D^{\left(n,s\right)}\right)^{-1}\left(\beta^{\ell}f\right)+c\beta^{s}e^{in\beta}.

(3.59)

In addition, if
s
and
s-\ell
have a same sign, the constant
c
should be zero.

Proof.

Since

\displaystyle D^{\left(n,s\right)}\Bigg(\underbrace{\beta^{\ell}\underbrace{\left(D^{\left(n,s-\ell\right)}\right)^{-1}f}_{\in C_{b}\text{ by assumption}}}_{\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\overline{\mathbb{R}}_{+})}-\underbrace{\left(D^{\left(n,s\right)}\right)^{-1}\left(\beta^{\ell}f\right)}_{\in C_{b}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\overline{\mathbb{R}}_{+})\text{ by assumption}}\Bigg)

\displaystyle{=}\beta^{\ell}D^{\left(n,s-\ell\right)}\left(D^{\left(n,s-\ell\right)}\right)^{-1}f-D^{\left(n,s\right)}\left(D^{\left(n,s\right)}\right)^{-1}\beta^{\ell}f

\displaystyle=\beta^{\ell}f-\beta^{\ell}f

\displaystyle=0,

\beta^{\ell}\left(D^{\left(n,s-\ell\right)}\right)^{-1}f-\left(D^{\left(n,s\right)}\right)^{-1}\left(\beta^{\ell}f\right)
lies in the kernel of the operator
D^{\left(n,s\right)}
. As the kernel of
D^{\left(n,s\right)}
is
\mathbb{C}\beta^{s}e^{in\beta}
as we saw in Proposition
3.20
, we get (
3.59
).

Dividing both side of (
3.59
) by
\beta^{s}e^{in\beta}
gives

e^{-in\beta}\beta^{\ell-s}\left(D^{\left(n,s-\ell\right)}\right)^{-1}f=e^{-in\beta}\beta^{-s}\left(D^{\left(n,s\right)}\right)^{-1}\left(\beta^{\ell}f\right)+c

Note here that
\left(D^{\left(n,s-\ell\right)}\right)^{-1}f
and
\left(D^{\left(n,s\right)}\right)^{-1}\left(\beta^{\ell}f\right)
are bounded by the assumption. If sgn(
s
) and sgn(
s-\ell
) are both positive,
\beta\rightarrow\infty
gives
c=0
. On the contrary, if sgn(
s
) and sgn(
s-\ell
) are both negative,
\beta\rightarrow 0
gives
c=0
.
∎

We are now ready to verify uniform boundedness of operator
id,Q,P^{(n)},P^{(n)}Q
, which form an each Fourier mode of differential bar operators (
3.35
)
\sim
(
3.41
) in
\overline{L}
. These results will be combined later to establish
C^{1}
continuity of
\overline{L}
.

Proposition 3.31
.

id:X^{(n)}_{0,N}\to W^{(n)}_{0,N}
is uniformly bounded. Furthermore, if
n\neq 0
, we have an exact norm bound

\left\|id\right\|_{\left[X^{(n)}_{0,N},W^{(n)}_{0,N}\right]}\leq\frac{\left\langle N\right\rangle}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\left(2\left(1+\frac{1}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\right)\left\|\beta\partial_{\beta}\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}+2\frac{\left\langle N\right\rangle}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\left\|\beta\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}+1\right)\cdot\frac{1}{\left\langle n\right\rangle}.

Proof.

For
n\notin N\mathbb{Z}
, nothing to be considered since norm is trivially zero, so we only treat the case
n\in N\mathbb{Z}
. As

\displaystyle\left\|id\right\|_{[X^{(n)}_{0,N},W^{(n)}_{0,N}]}

\displaystyle=\left\|id\right\|_{\left[\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}W^{(n)}_{0,N},W^{(n)}_{0,N}\right]}

\displaystyle=\left\|\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{[W^{(n)}_{0,N},W^{(n)}_{0,N}]}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{[C_{b,N}^{\delta},W^{(n)}_{0,N}]}+\left\|\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{[\mathbb{C}\xi_{0,N},W^{(n)}_{0,N}]}+\delta_{0n}\left\|\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{[\mathbb{C},W^{(n)}_{0,N}]},

norm bounds (
3.52
), (
3.53
),(
3.54
), (
3.57
), (
3.58
) give uniform boundedness.

For
n\neq 0
, we calculate norm bound more explicitly. As
\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}C_{b}^{\delta}\subset C_{b}^{\delta},\,\,\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}C_{b}^{\delta}\subset C_{b}^{\delta}\subset W^{(n)}_{0,N}
, we have

\left\|\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{[C_{b,N}^{\delta},W^{(n)}_{0,N}]}\leq\left\|\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}\right\|_{[C_{b,N}^{\delta},C_{b,N}^{\delta}]}\left\|\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{[C_{b,N}^{\delta},C_{b,N}^{\delta}]}\underset{\eqref{Pninv cbd},\eqref{Qinv cbd}}{=}2\frac{\left\langle N\right\rangle}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\cdot\frac{1}{\left\langle n\right\rangle}

Also, since
\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}\xi_{0}
could be calculated as

\displaystyle\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}\xi_{0}

\displaystyle{=}\frac{1}{(2-n)\mu-1}\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}\left(\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}(\beta\partial_{\beta}\xi_{0})-in\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}(\beta\xi_{0})-\xi_{0}\right)

\displaystyle{=}\frac{1}{(2-n)\mu-1}\left(\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}(\beta\partial_{\beta}\xi_{0})-in\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}(\beta\xi_{0})+\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}(\beta\partial_{\beta}\xi_{0})-\xi_{0}\right),

its norm can be estimated by

\displaystyle\left\|\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{[\mathbb{C}\xi_{0,N},C_{b,N}^{\delta}\oplus\mathbb{C}\xi_{0,N}]}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\left|(2-n)\mu-1\right|}\Bigg(\left\|\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}\right\|_{[C_{b,N}^{\delta},C_{b,N}^{\delta}]}\left(\left\|\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{[C_{b,N}^{\delta},C_{b,N}^{\delta}]}+1\right)\left\|\beta\partial_{\beta}\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b,N}^{\delta}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\left|n\right|\left\|\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}\right\|_{[C_{b,N}^{\delta},C_{b,N}^{\delta}]}\left\|\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{[C_{b,N}^{\delta},C_{b,N}^{\delta}]}\left\|\beta\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}+1\Bigg)

\displaystyle{\leq}\frac{\left\langle N\right\rangle}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\cdot\frac{1}{\left\langle n\right\rangle}\Bigg(2\left(\frac{\left\langle N\right\rangle}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\cdot\frac{1}{\left\langle n\right\rangle}+1\right)\left\|\beta\partial_{\beta}\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+2\left|n\right|\frac{\left\langle N\right\rangle}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\cdot\frac{1}{\left\langle n\right\rangle}\left\|\beta\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}+1\Bigg)

\displaystyle{\leq}\frac{\left\langle N\right\rangle}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\left(2\left(1+\frac{1}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\right)\left\|\beta\partial_{\beta}\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}+2\frac{\left\langle N\right\rangle}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\left\|\beta\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}+1\right)\cdot\frac{1}{\left\langle n\right\rangle}.

∎

Corollary 3.32
.

in\cdot id:X^{(n)}_{0,N}\rightarrow W^{(n)}_{-,N}
is uniformly bounded with an exact norm bound

\left\|in\cdot id\right\|_{\left[X^{(n)}_{0,N},W^{(n)}_{0,N}\right]}\leq\frac{\left\langle N\right\rangle}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\left(2\left(1+\frac{1}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\right)\left\|\beta\partial_{\beta}\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}+2\frac{\left\langle N\right\rangle}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\left\|\beta\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}+1\right).

Remark 3.33
.

Since the multiplier
in
kills the zero Fourier mode, the range of
in\cdot id
could be
W^{(n)}_{-,N}
.

Proposition 3.34
.

Q:X^{(n)}_{0,N}\rightarrow W^{(n)}_{-,N}
is uniformly bounded with an exact norm bound

\displaystyle\left\|Q\right\|_{[X^{(n)}_{0,N},W^{(n)}_{-,N}]}

\displaystyle\leq\Bigg(3\frac{\left\langle N\right\rangle}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}+\frac{\left\langle N\right\rangle}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\left(\frac{3}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}+2\right)\left\|\beta\partial_{\beta}\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}

\displaystyle\qquad+3\left(\frac{\left\langle N\right\rangle}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\right)^{2}\left\|\beta\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}\Bigg)\cdot\frac{1}{\left\langle n\right\rangle}.

Proof.

It suffices to focus on
n\in N\mathbb{Z}
, since the norm is 0 for
n\notin N\mathbb{Z}
. Note here that the range of
Q
is
W^{(n)}_{-,N}
, which is smaller than that of
id
. This is because, for a constant
c\in\mathbb{C}
,
\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}\left(P^{(0)}_{-}\right)^{-1}c
is a constant by Proposition
3.54
and
Q
is equal to
\beta\partial_{\beta}
, which kills a constant function. As before, we can estimate the norm of
Q:X^{(n)}_{0,N}\rightarrow W^{(n)}_{-,N}
when
n\in N\mathbb{Z}\backslash\{0\}
as follows;

\displaystyle\left\|Q\right\|_{[X^{(n)}_{0,N},W^{(n)}_{-,N}]}

\displaystyle=\left\|Q\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{[W^{(n)}_{0,N},W^{(n)}_{-,N}]}

\displaystyle=\left\|\left(Q+1-1\right)\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{[W^{(n)}_{0,N},W^{(n)}_{-,N}]}

\displaystyle=\left\|\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}-\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{[W^{(n)}_{0,N},W^{(n)}_{-,N}]}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}-\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{[C_{b,N}^{\delta},C_{b,N}^{\delta}]}+\left\|\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}-\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{[\mathbb{C}\xi_{0,N},C_{b,N}^{\delta}\oplus\mathbb{C}\xi_{0,N}]}.

By (
3.52
) and (
3.53
), we have

\displaystyle\left\|\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}-\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{[C_{b,N}^{\delta},C_{b,N}^{\delta}]}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\left\langle N\right\rangle}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\cdot\frac{1}{\left\langle n\right\rangle}+2\frac{\left\langle N\right\rangle}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\cdot\frac{1}{\left\langle n\right\rangle}

\displaystyle=3\frac{\left\langle N\right\rangle}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\cdot\frac{1}{\left\langle n\right\rangle}.

Also, from the formula

\displaystyle\left(\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}-\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}\right)\xi_{0}

\displaystyle{=}\frac{1}{(2-n)\mu-1}\Bigg(\underbrace{\left(\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}-\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}-\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}\right)(\beta\partial_{\beta}\xi_{0})}_{\underset{\text{Prop }\ref{D^{-1} in C_b^delta}}{\in}C_{b}^{\delta}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\underbrace{in\left(\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}-\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}\right)(\beta\xi_{0})}_{\underset{\text{Prop }\ref{D^{-1} in C_b^delta}}{\in}C_{b}^{\delta}}\Bigg),

we can estimate
\left\|\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}-\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{[\mathbb{C}\xi_{0,N},C_{b,N}^{\delta}\oplus\mathbb{C}\xi_{0,N}]}
by

\displaystyle\left\|\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}-\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{[\mathbb{C}\xi_{0,N},C_{b,N}^{\delta}\oplus\mathbb{C}\xi_{0,N}]}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\left|(2-n)\mu-1\right|}\Bigg(\left\|\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{[C_{b,N}^{\delta},C_{b,N}^{\delta}]}+\left\|\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}\right\|_{[C_{b,N}^{\delta},C_{b,N}^{\delta}]}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad+\left\|\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}\right\|_{[C_{b,N}^{\delta},C_{b,N}^{\delta}]}\left\|\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{[C_{b,N}^{\delta},C_{b,N}^{\delta}]}\Bigg)\left\|\beta\partial_{\beta}\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{\left|n\right|}{\left|(2-n)\mu-1\right|}\left(\left\|\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}\right\|_{[C_{b,N}^{\delta},C_{b,N}^{\delta}]}\left\|\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{[C_{b,N}^{\delta},C_{b,N}^{\delta}]}+\left\|\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{[C_{b,N}^{\delta},C_{b,N}^{\delta}]}\right)\left\|\beta\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}

\displaystyle{\leq}\frac{\left\langle N\right\rangle}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\cdot\frac{1}{\left\langle n\right\rangle}\left(3\frac{\left\langle N\right\rangle}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\cdot\frac{1}{\left\langle n\right\rangle}+2\right)\left\|\beta\partial_{\beta}\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\frac{\left\langle N\right\rangle}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\cdot\frac{\left|n\right|}{\left\langle n\right\rangle}\left(3\frac{\left\langle N\right\rangle}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\cdot\frac{1}{\left\langle n\right\rangle}\right)\left\|\beta\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{\left\langle N\right\rangle}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\left(\frac{3}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}+2\right)\left\|\beta\partial_{\beta}\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}+3\left(\frac{\left\langle N\right\rangle}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\right)^{2}\left\|\beta\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}\right)\cdot\frac{1}{\left\langle n\right\rangle}.

Combining these two estimates yields the result.
∎

Proposition 3.35
.

P^{(n)}(Q+1):X^{(n)}_{0,N}\rightarrow W^{(n)}_{-,N}
is uniformly bounded with an exact norm bound

\left\|P^{(n)}(Q+1)\right\|_{[X^{(n)}_{0,N},W^{(n)}_{-,N}]}\leq 4+\frac{\left\langle N\right\rangle}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\left\|\beta\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}+\frac{1}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\left\|\beta\partial_{\beta}\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}.

Proof.

As always, it suffices to focus on
n\in N\mathbb{Z}
. As in the case of
Q
, the range of
P^{(n)}(Q+1)
is contained in
W^{(n)}_{-,N}
. This is because, for a constant
c\in\mathbb{C}
,
\left(P^{(0)}_{-}\right)^{-1}c
is a constant by Proposition
3.26
and

P^{(0)}(Q+1)\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}\left(P^{(0)}_{-}\right)^{-1}c=\underbrace{P^{(0)}}_{=\beta\partial_{\beta}}\underbrace{\left(P^{(0)}_{-}\right)^{-1}c}_{\in\mathbb{C}}=0.

Now, we estimate the norm of
P^{(n)}(Q+1)
for
n\in N\mathbb{Z}\backslash\{0\}
.

\displaystyle\left\|P^{(n)}(Q+1)\right\|_{[X^{(n)}_{0,N},W^{(n)}_{-,N}]}

\displaystyle=\left\|P^{(n)}(Q+1)\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{[W^{(n)}_{0,N},W^{(n)}_{-,N}]}

\displaystyle=\left\|P^{(n)}\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{[W^{(n)}_{0,N},W^{(n)}_{-,N}]}

\displaystyle=\left\|\left(P^{(n)}_{-}+\left((2-n)\mu-1\right)\right)\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{[W^{(n)}_{0,N},W^{(n)}_{-,N}]}

\displaystyle{=}\left\|\left(P^{(n)}_{-}+\left((2-n)\mu-1\right)\right)\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{[W^{(n)}_{0,N},W^{(n)}_{0,N}]}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|id\right\|_{[W^{(n)}_{0,N},W^{(n)}_{0,N}]}+\left|(2-n)\mu-1\right|\left\|\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{[W^{(n)}_{0,N},W^{(n)}_{0,N}]}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|id\right\|_{[W^{(n)}_{0,N},W^{(n)}_{0,N}]}+\left|(2-n)\mu-1\right|\left\|\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{[C_{b,N}^{\delta},C_{b,N}^{\delta}]}+\left|(2-n)\mu-1\right|\left\|\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{[\mathbb{C}\xi_{0,N},C_{b,N}^{\delta}\oplus\mathbb{C}\xi_{0,N}]}

\displaystyle{\leq}1+\underbrace{\frac{\left|(2-n)\mu-1\right|}{\text{dist}([-\delta,\delta],(2-n)\mu-1)}}_{\leq 2}+\frac{\left\langle N\right\rangle}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\cdot\frac{1}{\left\langle n\right\rangle}\left(\left\|\beta\partial_{\beta}\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}+\left|n\right|\left\|\beta\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}\right)+1

\displaystyle\leq 4+\frac{\left\langle N\right\rangle}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\left\|\beta\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}+\frac{1}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\left\|\beta\partial_{\beta}\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}.

∎

Even though a differential operator might be expected to damage the regularity, we are safe so far. This is because
X^{(n)}_{0,N}
was made by taking inverse of differential operator, so even if we apply differential operator, it was cancelled out. However, when we deal with operator
P^{(n)}
, this cancelling does not occur directly which makes the proof harder. We first deal with how
P^{(n)}
acts on
C_{b}^{\delta}
.

Proposition 3.36
.

P^{(n)}\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}:C_{b,N}^{\delta}\rightarrow C_{b,N}^{\delta}\oplus\mathbb{C}\xi_{\infty,N}
is uniformly bounded. Furthermore, if
n\neq 0
, we have an exact norm bound

\displaystyle\left\|P^{(n)}\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{[C_{b,N}^{\delta},C_{b,N}^{\delta}\oplus\mathbb{C}\xi_{\infty,N}]}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4}{(N-2)\mu-1}+\frac{66}{5}+\left(\frac{\left\langle N\right\rangle+1}{(N-2)\mu-1}+\frac{66}{5}\right)\textnormal{max}\left\{\left\|\beta^{\delta}\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}},\left\|\beta^{-\delta}\xi_{\infty}\right\|_{C_{b}}\right\}

Proof.

Since
P^{(0)}=Q
and boundedness of
Q\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}\left(P^{(0)}_{-}\right)^{-1}
was shown in Proposition
3.34
, we focus on
n\in N\mathbb{Z}\backslash\{0\}
. As
P^{(n)}=Q-in\beta
and we already dealt with uniform boundedness of

Q\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}:C_{b,N}^{\delta}\oplus\mathbb{C}\xi_{0,N}\rightarrow C_{b,N}^{\delta}

in Proposition
3.34
, we analyze

in\beta\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}:C_{b,N}^{\delta}\oplus\mathbb{C}\xi_{0,N}\rightarrow C_{b,N}^{\delta}\oplus\mathbb{C}\xi_{\infty,N}.

For
f\in C_{b}^{\delta}
,

\displaystyle in\beta\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}f

(3.60)

\displaystyle=in\beta\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}\underbrace{\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}\beta^{\delta}\beta^{-\delta}f}_{(*)}

\displaystyle\underset{\eqref{comm dnsi}}{}

\displaystyle{\displaystyle=}in\beta\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}\underbrace{\beta^{\delta}\left(P^{(n)}_{-}+\delta\right)^{-1}\beta^{-\delta}f}_{(*)}

\displaystyle=\beta^{\delta}\underbrace{in\beta^{1-\delta}\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}\beta^{\delta}\left(P^{(n)}_{-}-\delta\right)^{-1}\beta^{-\delta}f}_{(**)}

\displaystyle\underset{\eqref{comm dnsi}}{}

\displaystyle{\displaystyle=}\beta^{\delta}\underbrace{\left(Q+\delta\right)^{-1}in\beta\left(P^{(n)}_{-}+\delta\right)^{-1}\beta^{-\delta}f}_{(**)}.

We explain how the second and the last equality are justified by checking assumptions in Proposition
3.30
. For second equality, note that
\beta^{-\delta}f\in C_{b}
and
\left(P^{(n)}_{-}+\delta\right)^{-1}\beta^{\delta}f\in C_{b}
as
f\in C_{b}^{\delta}
. Also, by (
3.50
),
\left((2-n)\mu-1\right)
and
\left((2-n)\mu-1-\delta\right)
are not equal to zero. Therefore, the assumption of Proposition
3.30
are satisfied. Also, the constant term
c
does not appear as
{\mathrm{sgn}}((2-n)\mu-1)={\mathrm{sgn}}((2-n)\mu-1-\delta)
.

For the last equality, we check whether
\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}\beta^{\delta}\left(P^{(n)}_{-}+\delta\right)^{-1}\beta^{-\delta}f
lies in
C_{b}
. This is clear as

\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}\beta^{\delta}\left(P^{(n)}_{-}+\delta\right)^{-1}\beta^{-\delta}f=\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}f\underset{\text{Prop }\ref{D^{-1} in C_b^delta}}{\in}C_{b}^{\delta}\hookrightarrow C_{b}

Second, we check whether
\left(Q+\delta\right)^{-1}in\beta\left(P^{(n)}_{-}+\delta\right)^{-1}\beta^{-\delta}f
is contained in
C_{b}
. The key is to write
in\beta
as

in\beta=(Q+\delta)-\left(P^{(n)}_{-}+\delta\right)-\left((2-n)\mu-1\right).

(3.61)

Then, we can enjoy the cancellation so that

\displaystyle\left(Q+\delta\right)^{-1}in\beta\left(P^{(n)}_{-}+\delta\right)^{-1}\beta^{-\delta}f

\displaystyle=\left(Q+\delta\right)^{-1}\left((Q+\delta)-\left(P^{(n)}_{-}+\delta\right)-((2-n)\mu-1)\right)\left(P^{(n)}_{-}+\delta\right)^{-1}\beta^{-\delta}f

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\left(P^{(n)}_{-}+\delta\right)^{-1}\underbrace{\beta^{-\delta}f}_{\in C_{b}(\because f\in C_{b}^{\delta})}}_{\underset{\text{Prop }\ref{D^{-1} in C_b}}{\in}C_{b}}-\underbrace{(Q+\delta)^{-1}\underbrace{\beta^{-\delta}f}_{\in C_{b}}}_{\underset{\text{Prop }\ref{D^{-1} in C_b}}{\in}C_{b}}-\underbrace{((2-n)\mu-1)(Q+\delta)^{-1}\left(P^{(n)}_{-}+\delta\right)^{-1}\underbrace{\beta^{-\delta}f}_{\in C_{b}}}_{\underset{\text{Prop }\ref{D^{-1} in C_b}}{\in}C_{b}}.

Third,
-1
and
-\delta
have same sign, so constant term
c
does not appear. This justified the last equality.

Since
(**)\in C_{b}
,
\beta^{\delta}
decay near
\beta=0
is now guaranteed in

in\beta\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}f=\beta(**)

But what happens near
\beta=\infty
? Repeating similar argument with little change, we have

\displaystyle in\beta\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}f

(3.62)

\displaystyle=in\beta\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}\underbrace{\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}\beta^{-\delta}\beta^{\delta}f}_{(*)}

\displaystyle\underset{\eqref{comm dnsi}}{}

\displaystyle{\displaystyle=}in\beta\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}\underbrace{\beta^{-\delta}\left(P^{(n)}_{-}-\delta\right)^{-1}\beta^{\delta}f}_{(*)}

\displaystyle=\beta^{-\delta}\Big(\underbrace{in\beta^{1+\delta}\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}\beta^{-\delta}\left(P^{(n)}_{-}-\delta\right)^{-1}\beta^{\delta}f}_{(***)}\Big)

\displaystyle\underset{\eqref{comm dnsi}}{}

\displaystyle{\displaystyle=}\beta^{-\delta}\Big(\underbrace{(Q-\delta)^{-1}in\beta\left(P^{(n)}_{-}-\delta\right)^{-1}\beta^{\delta}f+c\beta^{\delta}}_{(***)}\Big)

\displaystyle=\beta^{-\delta}\underbrace{(Q-\delta)^{-1}in\beta\left(P^{(n)}_{-}-\delta\right)^{-1}\beta^{\delta}f}_{(****)}+c.

Checking the assumption of Proposition
3.30
is similar to above, so we omit it. However, a big difference is that in the second application of Proposition
3.30
, the sign of
-1
and
\delta
is different, so constant term
c
appears. Since the term
(****)
belongs to
C_{b}
for the same reason as
(**)
belongs to
C_{b}
,

in\beta\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}f-c

shows
\beta^{-\delta}
decay at
\beta=\infty
. Hence, to estimate
C_{b}^{\delta}\oplus\mathbb{C}\xi_{\infty}
norm of
in\beta\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}P^{(n)}_{-}f
, we need to estimate a constant
c
first. From (
3.62
), we have

c=in\beta\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}f-\beta^{-\delta}(Q-\delta)^{-1}in\beta\left(P^{(n)}_{-}-\delta\right)^{-1}\beta^{\delta}f.

Take
\beta=1
and decomposing
in\beta
to

in\beta=(Q-\delta)-\left(P^{(n)}_{-}-\delta\right)-((2-n)\mu-1)

(3.63)

gives

\displaystyle c=

\displaystyle in\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}f

\displaystyle-\left(P^{(n)}_{-}-\delta\right)^{-1}\beta^{\delta}f

\displaystyle+(Q-\delta)^{-1}\beta^{\delta}f

\displaystyle+((2-n)\mu-1)\left(Q-\delta\right)^{-1}\left(P^{(n)}_{-}-\delta\right)^{-1}\beta^{\delta}f

\displaystyle\eqqcolon I_{1}+I_{2}+I_{3}+I_{4}.

Let us estimate upper bound of
I_{1}\sim I_{4}
one by one.

\displaystyle\left\|I_{1}\right\|_{C_{b}}

\displaystyle=\left|n\right|\left\|\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}f\right\|_{C_{b}}

\displaystyle{\leq}\frac{\left|n\right|}{\mathrm{dist}(\{0\},(2-n)\mu-1)}\left\|f\right\|_{C_{b}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\left|n\right|}{\mathrm{dist}([-\delta,\delta],(2-n)\mu-1)}\left\|f\right\|_{C_{b}}

\displaystyle{\leq}\left|n\right|\frac{\left\langle N\right\rangle}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\cdot\frac{1}{\left\langle n\right\rangle}\left\|f\right\|_{C_{b}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\left\langle N\right\rangle}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\left\|f\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}},

\displaystyle\left\|I_{2}\right\|_{C_{b}}

\displaystyle=\left\|\left(P^{(n)}_{-}-\delta\right)^{-1}\beta^{\delta}f\right\|_{C_{b}}

\displaystyle{\leq}\frac{1}{\left|(2-n)\mu-1+\delta\right|}\left\|\beta^{\delta}f\right\|_{C_{b}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\text{dist}([-\delta,\delta],(2-n)\mu-1)}\left\|f\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}

\displaystyle{\leq}\frac{\left\langle N\right\rangle}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\cdot\frac{1}{\left\langle n\right\rangle}\left\|f\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}

\displaystyle{\leq}\frac{1}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\left\|f\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}},

\displaystyle\left\|I_{3}\right\|_{C_{b}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|(Q-\delta)^{-1}\beta^{\delta}f\right\|_{C_{b}}

\displaystyle{\leq}\frac{1}{\delta}\left\|\beta^{\delta}f\right\|_{C_{b}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\delta}\left\|f\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}

\displaystyle{\leq}6\left\|f\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}},

\displaystyle\left\|I_{4}\right\|_{C_{b}}

\displaystyle=\left\|((2-n)\mu-1)(Q-\delta)^{-1}\left(P^{(n)}_{-}-\delta\right)^{-1}\beta^{\delta}f\right\|_{C_{b}}

\displaystyle{\leq}\left|(2-n)\mu-1\right|\cdot\frac{1}{\delta}\cdot\frac{1}{\text{dist}([-\delta,\delta],(2-n)\mu-1)}\left\|f\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\delta}\cdot\frac{1}{\mathrm{dist}([-\delta,\delta],-1)}\left\|f\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}

\displaystyle{\leq}\frac{36}{5}\left\|f\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}.

Combining these result, we have

\displaystyle\left|c\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{\left\langle N\right\rangle+1}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}+\frac{66}{5}\right)\left\|f\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}.

(3.64)

As we have estimated
\mathbb{C}\xi_{\infty}
part of
in\beta\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}f
, we now estimate
C_{b}^{\delta}
norm of
in\beta\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}f-c\xi_{\infty}
. First,

\displaystyle\left\|\beta^{\delta}\left(in\beta\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}f-c\xi_{\infty}\right)\right\|_{C_{b}}

\displaystyle{=}\left\|\beta^{\delta}\left(\beta^{-\delta}(Q-\delta)^{-1}in\beta\left(P^{(n)}_{-}-\delta\right)^{-1}\beta^{\delta}f+c(1-\xi_{\infty})\right)\right\|_{C_{b}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|(Q-\delta)^{-1}in\beta\left(P^{(n)}_{-}-\delta\right)^{-1}\beta^{\delta}f\right\|_{C_{b}}+\left|c\right|\left\|\beta^{\delta}\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}}

\displaystyle{\leq}\left\|I_{2}\right\|_{C_{b}}+\left\|I_{3}\right\|_{C_{b}}+\left\|I_{4}\right\|_{C_{b}}+\left|c\right|\left\|\beta^{\delta}\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}}

\displaystyle{\leq}\left(\frac{1}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}+\frac{66}{5}+\left(\frac{\left\langle N\right\rangle+1}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}+\frac{66}{5}\right)\left\|\beta^{\delta}\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}}\right)\left\|f\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}.

Second,

\displaystyle\left\|\beta^{-\delta}\left(in\beta\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}f-c\xi_{\infty}\right)\right\|_{C_{b}}

\displaystyle{=}\left\|\beta^{-\delta}\left(\beta^{\delta}(Q+\delta)^{-1}in\beta\left(P^{(n)}_{-}+\delta\right)^{-1}\beta^{-\delta}f-c\xi_{\infty}\right)\right\|_{C_{b}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|(Q+\delta)^{-1}in\beta\left(P^{(n)}_{-}+\delta\right)^{-1}\beta^{-\delta}f\right\|_{C_{b}}+\left|c\right|\left\|\beta^{-\delta}\xi_{\infty}\right\|_{C_{b}}

\displaystyle{\leq}\left\|\left(P^{(n)}_{-}+\delta\right)^{-1}\beta^{-\delta}f\right\|_{C_{b}}+\left\|(Q+\delta)^{-1}\beta^{-\delta}f\right\|_{C_{b}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\left|(2-n)\mu-1\right|\left\|(Q+\delta)^{-1}\left(P^{(n)}_{-}+\delta\right)^{-1}\beta^{-\delta}f\right\|_{C_{b}}+\left|c\right|\left\|\beta^{-\delta}\xi_{\infty}\right\|_{C_{b}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{1}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}+\frac{66}{5}+\left(\frac{\left\langle N\right\rangle+1}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}+\frac{66}{5}\right)\left\|\beta^{-\delta}\xi_{\infty}\right\|_{C_{b}}\right)\left\|f\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}.

Combining these result, we have

\displaystyle\left\|in\beta\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{[C_{b,N}^{\delta},C_{b,N}^{\delta}\oplus\mathbb{C}\xi_{\infty,N}]}

(3.65)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}+\frac{66}{5}+\left(\frac{\left\langle N\right\rangle+1}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}+\frac{66}{5}\right)\textnormal{max}\left\{\left\|\beta^{\delta}\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}},\left\|\beta^{-\delta}\xi_{\infty}\right\|_{C_{b}}\right\}.

Then,

\displaystyle\left\|P^{(n)}\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{[C_{b,N}^{\delta},C_{b,N}^{\delta}\oplus\mathbb{C}\xi_{\infty,N}]}

\displaystyle{\leq}\left\|(Q-in\beta)\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{[C_{b,N}^{\delta},C_{b,N}^{\delta}\oplus\mathbb{C}\xi_{\infty,N}]}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|Q\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{[C_{b,N}^{\delta},C_{b,N}^{\delta}]}+\left\|in\beta\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{[C_{b,N}^{\delta},C_{b,N}^{\delta}\oplus\mathbb{C}\xi_{\infty,N}]}

\displaystyle{\leq}3\frac{\left\langle N\right\rangle}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\cdot\frac{1}{\left\langle n\right\rangle}+\frac{1}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}+\frac{66}{5}+\left(\frac{\left\langle N\right\rangle+1}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}+\frac{66}{5}\right)\textnormal{max}\left\{\left\|\beta^{\delta}\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}},\left\|\beta^{-\delta}\xi_{\infty}\right\|_{C_{b}}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}+\frac{66}{5}+\left(\frac{\left\langle N\right\rangle+1}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}+\frac{66}{5}\right)\textnormal{max}\left\{\left\|\beta^{\delta}\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}},\left\|\beta^{-\delta}\xi_{\infty}\right\|_{C_{b}}\right\}.

∎

Proposition 3.37
.

P^{(n)}\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}:\mathbb{C}\xi_{0,N}\rightarrow C_{b,N}^{\delta}\oplus\mathbb{C}\xi_{\infty,N}
is uniformly bounded. Furthermore, if
n\neq 0
, we have an exact norm bound

\displaystyle\left\|P^{(n)}\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{[\mathbb{C}\xi_{0,N},C_{b,N}^{\delta}\oplus\mathbb{C}\xi_{\infty,N}]}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{4}{(N-2)\mu-1}+\frac{66}{5}+\left(\frac{\left\langle N\right\rangle+1}{(N-2)\mu-1}+\frac{66}{5}\right)\textnormal{max}\left\{\left\|\beta^{\delta}\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}},\left\|\beta^{-\delta}\xi_{\infty}\right\|_{C_{b}}\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\Bigg(\left(1+\frac{2}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\right)\left\|\beta\partial_{\beta}\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}+\frac{\left\langle N\right\rangle}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\left\|\beta\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}+\frac{\left\langle N\right\rangle}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\left\|\beta^{2}\partial_{\beta}\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad+\frac{1}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\left\|\beta^{2}\partial^{2}_{\beta}\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}\Bigg)+\frac{1}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\left\|\beta\partial_{\beta}\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}+\frac{\left\langle N\right\rangle}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\left\|\beta\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}.

Proof.

Since
P^{(0)}=Q
for
n=0
and this case is was already dealt with, we only prove for
n\in N\mathbb{Z}\backslash\{0\}
. Direct calculation using (
3.56
) gives

\displaystyle P^{(n)}\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}\xi_{0}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(2-n)\mu-1}\underbrace{P^{(n)}\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}\bigg(\underbrace{\Big(2-((2-n)\mu-1)\Big)\beta\partial_{\beta}\xi_{0}-in\beta\xi_{0}-in\beta^{2}\partial_{\beta}\xi_{0}+\beta^{2}\partial^{2}_{\beta}\xi_{0}}_{\in C_{b}^{\delta}}\bigg)}_{\underset{\text{Prop }\ref{continuity of Pn in cbd}}{\in}C_{b}^{\delta}\oplus\mathbb{C}\xi_{\infty}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{(2-n)\mu-1}(\underbrace{-\beta\partial_{\beta}\xi_{0}+in\beta_{0}}_{\in C_{b}^{\delta}}).

Then,
C_{b,N}^{\delta}\oplus\mathbb{C}\xi_{\infty,N}
norm of it could be bounded by

\displaystyle\left\|P^{(n)}\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b,N}^{\delta}\oplus\mathbb{C}\xi_{\infty,N}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\left|(2-n)\mu-1\right|}\left\|P^{(n)}\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{[C_{b,N}^{\delta},C_{b,N}^{\delta}\oplus\mathbb{C}\xi_{\infty,N}]}

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\left\|\Big(2-((2-n)\mu-1)\Big)\beta\partial_{\beta}\xi_{0}-in\beta\xi_{0}-in\beta^{2}\partial_{\beta}\xi_{0}+\beta^{2}\partial^{2}_{\beta}\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{\left|(2-n)\mu-1\right|}\left\|-\beta\partial_{\beta}\xi_{0}+in\beta\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{4}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}+\frac{66}{5}+\left(\frac{\left\langle N\right\rangle+1}{(N-2)\mu-1}+\frac{66}{5}\right)\textnormal{max}\left\{\left\|\beta^{\delta}\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}},\left\|\beta^{-\delta}\xi_{\infty}\right\|_{C_{b}}\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\Bigg(\left(1+\frac{2}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\right)\left\|\beta\partial_{\beta}\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}+\frac{\left\langle N\right\rangle}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\left\|\beta\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}+\frac{\left\langle N\right\rangle}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\left\|\beta^{2}\partial_{\beta}\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad+\frac{1}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\left\|\beta^{2}\partial^{2}_{\beta}\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}\Bigg)+\frac{1}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\left\|\beta\partial_{\beta}\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}+\frac{\left\langle N\right\rangle}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\left\|\beta\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}

∎

Combining Proposition
3.36
and Proposition
3.37
yields the continuity of
P^{(n)}
as follows.

Proposition 3.38
.

P^{(n)}:X^{(n)}_{0,N}\rightarrow W^{(n)}_{+,N}
is uniformly bounded. Furthermore, if
n\neq 0
, we have exact norm bound

\displaystyle\left\|P^{(n)}\right\|_{[X^{(n)}_{0,N},W^{(n)}_{+,N}]}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{4}{(N-2)\mu-1}+\frac{66}{5}+\left(\frac{\left\langle N\right\rangle+1}{(N-2)\mu-1}+\frac{66}{5}\right)\textnormal{max}\left\{\left\|\beta^{\delta}\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}},\left\|\beta^{-\delta}\xi_{\infty}\right\|_{C_{b}}\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\Bigg(\left(1+\frac{2}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\right)\left\|\beta\partial_{\beta}\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}+\frac{\left\langle N\right\rangle}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\left\|\beta\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}+\frac{\left\langle N\right\rangle}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\left\|\beta^{2}\partial_{\beta}\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad+\frac{1}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\left\|\beta^{2}\partial^{2}_{\beta}\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}+1\Bigg)+\frac{1}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\left\|\beta\partial_{\beta}\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}+\frac{\left\langle N\right\rangle}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\left\|\beta\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}.

Proof.

\displaystyle\left\|P^{(n)}\right\|_{[X^{(n)}_{0,N},W^{(n)}_{+,N}]}=\left\|P^{(n)}\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{[W^{(n)}_{0,N},W^{(n)}_{+,N}]}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|P^{(n)}\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{[C_{b,N}^{\delta},W^{(n)}_{+,N}]}+\left\|P^{(n)}\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{[\mathbb{C}\xi_{0,N},W^{(n)}_{+,N}]}.

∎

Proposition 3.39
.

P^{(n)}Q:X^{(n)}_{0,N}\rightarrow W^{(n)}_{+,N}
is uniformly bounded. Furthermore, if
n\neq 0
, we have exact norm bound

\displaystyle\left\|P^{(n)}Q\right\|_{[X^{(n)}_{0,N},W^{(n)}_{+,N}]}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{4}{(N-2)\mu-1}+\frac{66}{5}+\left(\frac{\left\langle N\right\rangle+1}{(N-2)\mu-1}+\frac{66}{5}\right)\textnormal{max}\left\{\left\|\beta^{\delta}\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}},\left\|\beta^{-\delta}\xi_{\infty}\right\|_{C_{b}}\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\Bigg(\left(1+\frac{2}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\right)\left\|\beta\partial_{\beta}\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}+\frac{\left\langle N\right\rangle}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\left\|\beta\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}+\frac{\left\langle N\right\rangle}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\left\|\beta^{2}\partial_{\beta}\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad+\frac{1}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\left\|\beta^{2}\partial^{2}_{\beta}\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}+1\Bigg)+\frac{2}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\left\|\beta\partial_{\beta}\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}+\frac{2\left\langle N\right\rangle}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\left\|\beta\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}+2.

Proof.

Apply Proposition
3.35
and Proposition
3.38
on

\displaystyle\left\|P^{(n)}Q\right\|_{[X^{(n)}_{0,N},W^{(n)}_{+,N}]}=\left\|P^{(n)}(Q+1)-P^{(n)}\right\|_{[X^{(n)}_{0,N},W^{(n)}_{+,N}]}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|P^{(n)}(Q+1)\right\|_{[X^{(n)}_{0,N},W^{(n)}_{-,N}]}+\left\|P^{(n)}\right\|_{[X^{(n)}_{0,N},W^{(n)}_{+,N}]}.

∎

So far, we have proved

\displaystyle id:X^{(n)}_{0,N}\rightarrow W^{(n)}_{0,N},\quad in\cdot id

\displaystyle:X^{(n)}_{0,N}\rightarrow W^{(n)}_{0,N},\quad Q:X^{(n)}_{0,N}\rightarrow W^{(n)}_{-,N},

(3.66)

\displaystyle P^{(n)}(Q+1):X^{(n)}_{0,N}\rightarrow W^{(n)}_{-,N},\quad P^{(n)}

\displaystyle:X^{(n)}_{0,N}\rightarrow W^{(n)}_{+,N},\quad P^{(n)}Q:X^{(n)}_{0,N}\rightarrow W^{(n)}_{+,N}

are uniformly bounded with explicit norm bound for
n\neq 0
. As
\partial_{\phi}
is induced by
in\cdot id
,
\beta\partial_{\beta}
is induced by
Q
,
\beta\partial_{\varphi}(\beta\partial_{\beta}+1)
is induced by
-P^{(n)}(Q+1)
,
\beta\partial_{\varphi}
is induced by
-P^{(n)}
and
\beta\partial_{\varphi}\beta\partial_{\beta}
is induced by
-P^{(n)}Q
, we can get the following proposition about the continuity of differential operators.

Proposition 3.40
.

\displaystyle id

\displaystyle:X_{0,N}\rightarrow W_{0,N},

\displaystyle\partial_{\phi}

\displaystyle:X_{0,N}\rightarrow W_{0,N},

\displaystyle\beta\partial_{\beta}

\displaystyle:X_{0,N}\rightarrow W_{-,N},

\displaystyle\beta\partial_{\varphi}

\displaystyle:X_{0,N}\rightarrow W_{+,N},

\displaystyle\beta\partial_{\varphi}(\beta\partial_{\beta}+1)

\displaystyle:X_{0,N},\rightarrow W_{-,N},

\displaystyle\beta\partial_{\varphi}\beta\partial_{\beta}

\displaystyle:X_{0,N}\rightarrow W_{+,N}

is
C^{1}
.

Proof.

As all operator in Proposition
3.40
is linear, its Fréchet derivative is same as itself. Then, boundedness of these operators follow directly by uniform boundedness of (
3.66
) and Proposition
3.13
.
∎

As each Fourier mode of differential bard operator in (
3.37
)
\sim
(
3.41
) is linear combination of operator in (
3.66
), the following proposition about differential bar operator follows directly.

Proposition 3.41
.

\displaystyle\overline{\partial}_{\beta}

\displaystyle:X_{0,N}\rightarrow W_{0,N},

\displaystyle\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}

\displaystyle:X_{0,N}\rightarrow W_{+,N},

\displaystyle(\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}+1)\overline{\partial}_{\beta}

\displaystyle:X_{0,N}\rightarrow W_{+,N},

\displaystyle\partial_{\phi}\overline{\partial}_{\beta}

\displaystyle:X_{0,N}\rightarrow W_{0,N}

is
C^{1}

Remember, our goal is to show that
\overline{L}
given by

\displaystyle\overline{L}(\overline{\psi},\Omega)=\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}\left(\frac{2\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}\cdot\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}\overline{\psi}}{\left(\bar{\partial}_{\varphi}+1\right)\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}}\left(1+\left(\frac{\partial_{\phi}\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}}{2\overline{\partial}_{\beta}}\right)^{2}\right)-\frac{\partial_{\phi}\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}\cdot\partial_{\phi}\overline{\psi}}{2\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\partial_{\phi}\left(\frac{\left(\bar{\partial}_{\varphi}+1\right)\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}\cdot\partial_{\phi}\overline{\psi}-\partial_{\phi}\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}\cdot\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}\overline{\psi}}{2\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}}\right)+\frac{\left(\bar{\partial}_{\varphi}+1\right)\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}\cdot\left(\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}\overline{\psi}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2\mu}}}{2\mu}\Omega

is
C^{1}
. For this aim, we need to avoid zero value in the denominators in (
3.1
). First, note that trivial solution
\overline{\psi}_{0}
is a constant function so that only zeroth Fourier mode is non-trivial. Therefore,

\overline{\psi}_{0}\in X_{0,N}.

Since
\overline{\partial}_{\beta}:X_{0,N}\rightarrow W_{0,N}
is continuous by Proposition
3.41
and
\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}_{0}\equiv-1\in X_{0,N}
, there exists
\delta_{1}>0
such that

\overline{\psi}\in B_{X_{0,N}}(\overline{\psi}_{0},\delta_{1})\Rightarrow\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}\in B_{W_{0,N}}\left(-1,\frac{1}{2}\right).

As

\left\|\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}-(-1)\right\|_{W_{0,N}}\underset{\text{def}}{=}\left\|\left(\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}\right)^{(0)}-(-1)\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}\oplus\mathbb{C}\xi_{0}\oplus\mathbb{C}}+\sum_{n\in N\mathbb{Z}\backslash\{0\}}\left\langle n\right\rangle^{0.5}\left\|\left(\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}\right)^{(n)}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}\oplus\mathbb{C}\xi_{0}}<\frac{1}{2}

and

C_{b}^{\delta}\hookrightarrow C_{b},\qquad C_{b}^{\delta}\oplus\mathbb{C}\xi_{0}\oplus\mathbb{C}\hookrightarrow C_{b}

with

\left\|f\right\|_{C_{b}}\leq\left\|f\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}\oplus\mathbb{C}\xi_{0}\oplus\mathbb{C}},\qquad\left\|f\right\|_{C_{b}}\leq\left\|f\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}},

we have

\left\|\left(\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}\right)^{(0)}-(-1)\right\|_{C_{b}}+\sum_{n\in N\mathbb{Z}\backslash\{0\}}\left\|\left(\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}\right)^{(n)}\right\|_{C_{b}}<\frac{1}{2}.

Then,

\left\|\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}-(-1)\right\|_{C_{b}\left(\overline{\mathbb{R}}_{+}\times\mathbb{T}\right)}\leq\sum_{n\in N\mathbb{Z}}\left\|\left(\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}\right)^{(n)}-(-1)^{(n)}\right\|_{C_{b}}<\frac{1}{2},

(3.67)

so that
\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}
is safely away from zero for
\overline{\psi}\in B_{X_{0,N}}\left(\overline{\psi}_{0},\delta_{1}\right)
.

Similarly, as
\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}:X_{0,N}\rightarrow W_{+,N}
is continuous by Proposition
3.41
and
\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}\overline{\psi}_{0}\equiv 1
, there exists
\delta_{2}>0
such that

\overline{\psi}\in B_{X_{0,N}}\left(\overline{\psi}_{0},\delta_{2}\right)\Rightarrow\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}\overline{\psi}\in B_{W_{+,N}}\left(1,\frac{1}{2}\right)

and

\left\|\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}\overline{\psi}-1\right\|_{C_{b}\left(\overline{\mathbb{R}}_{+}\times\mathbb{T}\right)}<\frac{1}{2}\qquad\text{for}\qquad\overline{\psi}\in B_{X_{0,N}}\left(\overline{\psi}_{0},\delta_{2}\right).

(3.68)

Also, since
(\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}+1)\overline{\partial}_{\beta}:X_{0,N}\rightarrow W_{+,N}
is continuous with
(\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}+1)\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}_{0}=-2\mu
, there exists
\delta_{3}>0
such that

\overline{\psi}\in B_{X_{0,N}}\left(\overline{\psi}_{0},\delta_{3}\right)\Rightarrow(\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}+1)\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}\in B_{W_{+,N}}\left(-2\mu,\mu\right)

and

\left\|(\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}+1)\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}-(-2\mu)\right\|_{C_{b}\left(\overline{\mathbb{R}}_{+}\times\mathbb{T}\right)}<\mu\qquad\text{for}\qquad\overline{\psi}\in B_{X_{0,N}}\left(\overline{\psi}_{0},\delta_{3}\right).

(3.69)

Although we have avoided zero-value in denominator through (
3.67
), (
3.68
), and (
3.69
), we continue to bound
\partial_{\phi}\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}
and
\overline{\psi}
for the future aim. Due to the fact that
\partial_{\phi},\partial_{\phi}\overline{\partial}_{\beta}:X_{0,N}\rightarrow W_{0,N}
is continuous with
\partial_{\phi}\overline{\psi}_{0}=\partial_{\phi}\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}_{0}\equiv 0
, there exists
\delta_{4}>0
such that

\overline{\psi}\in B_{X_{0,N}}\left(\overline{\psi},\delta_{4}\right)\Rightarrow\partial_{\phi}\overline{\psi},\partial_{\phi}\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}\in B_{W_{0,N}}\left(0,1\right)

and

\textnormal{max}\left\{\left\|\partial_{\phi}\overline{\psi}\right\|_{C_{b}\left(\overline{\mathbb{R}}_{+}\times\mathbb{T}\right)},\left\|\partial_{\phi}\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}\right\|_{C_{b}\left(\overline{\mathbb{R}}_{+}\times\mathbb{T}\right)}\right\}<1\qquad\text{for}\qquad\overline{\psi}\in B_{X_{0,N}}\left(\overline{\psi}_{0},\delta_{4}\right)

(3.70)

Lastly, since
id:X_{0,N}\rightarrow W_{0,N}
is continuous with
\overline{\psi}_{0}\equiv\frac{1}{2\mu-1}
, there exists
\delta_{5}>0
such that

\overline{\psi}\in B_{X_{0,N}}\left(\overline{\psi}_{0},\delta_{5}\right)\Rightarrow\overline{\psi}_{0}\in B_{W_{0,N}}\left(\frac{1}{2\mu-1},\frac{1}{4\mu-2}\right)

and

\left\|\overline{\psi}-\frac{1}{2\mu-1}\right\|_{C_{b}\left(\overline{\mathbb{R}}_{+}\times\mathbb{T}\right)}\leq\frac{1}{4\mu-2}\qquad\text{for}\qquad\overline{\psi}\in B_{X_{0,N}}\left(\overline{\psi}_{0},\delta_{5}\right).

(3.71)

Now that we have made denominator in
\overline{L}
strictly away from zero, we will get a little more closer to
C^{1}
continuity.

Proposition 3.42
.

There exists
\delta^{\prime}_{1}>0
such that
\delta^{\prime}_{1}<\delta_{1}
and a map

\frac{1}{\overline{\partial}_{\beta}}:B_{X_{0,N}}\left(\overline{\psi}_{0},\delta^{\prime}_{1}\right)\rightarrow W_{0,N}

defined by
\frac{1}{\overline{\partial}_{\beta}}\left(\overline{\psi}\right)=\frac{1}{\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}}
is
C^{1}
.

Proof.

By Proposition
3.11
and
3.18
,
W_{0,N}
is a Banach algebra. Since
W_{0,N}
contains constant function,
W_{0,N}
is an unital Banach algebra. Then, there exists
\varepsilon_{1}>0
such that inverse map

\text{inv}:f(\beta,\phi)\mapsto\frac{1}{f(\beta,\phi)}

is analytic on
B_{W_{0,N}}\left(-1,\varepsilon_{1}\right)
. Since

\overline{\partial}_{\beta}:X_{0,N}\rightarrow W_{0,N}

is continuous, there exists
\delta^{\prime}_{1}<\delta_{1}
, where
\delta_{1}
is as in (
3.67
), such that

\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\left(B_{X_{0,N}}\left(\overline{\psi}_{0},\delta^{\prime}_{1}\right)\right)\subset B_{W_{0,N}}\left(-1,\varepsilon_{1}\right).

Then,

\text{inv}\circ\overline{\partial}_{\beta}:B_{X_{0,N}}\left(\overline{\psi}_{0},\delta^{\prime}_{1}\right)\rightarrow W_{0,N}

is a desired map.
∎

Proposition 3.43
.

There exists
\delta^{\prime}_{2}>0
such that
\delta^{\prime}_{2}<\delta_{2}
and two maps

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}}

\displaystyle:B_{X_{0,N}}\left(\overline{\psi}_{0},\delta^{\prime}_{2}\right)\rightarrow W_{+,N},

\displaystyle\left(\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2\mu}}

\displaystyle:B_{X_{0,N}}\left(\overline{\psi}_{0},\delta^{\prime}_{2}\right)\rightarrow W_{+,N}

defined by

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}}\left(\overline{\psi}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}\overline{\psi}},

\displaystyle\left(\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2\mu}}\left(\overline{\psi}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}\overline{\psi}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2\mu}}

is
C^{1}

Proof.

Use same method in Proposition
3.42
.
∎

Proposition 3.44
.

There exists
\delta^{\prime}_{3}>0
such that
\delta^{\prime}_{3}<\delta_{3}
and a map

\frac{\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}}{(\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}+1)\overline{\partial}_{\beta}}:B_{X_{0,N}}\left(\overline{\psi}_{0},\delta^{\prime}_{3}\right)\rightarrow W_{0,N}

defined by

\left(\frac{\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}}{(\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}+1)\overline{\partial}_{\beta}}\right)\left(\overline{\psi}\right)=\frac{\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}\overline{\psi}}{(\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}+1)\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}}

is
C^{1}

Proof.

Focus on the range of the map. This is very interesting. Since, as can be seen in Proposition
3.41
, the range of
\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}
and
(\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}+1)\overline{\partial}_{\beta}
is
W_{+,N}
, it seems natural that the range of
\frac{\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}}{(\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}+1)\overline{\partial}_{\beta}}
be the
W_{+,N}
. Let us see why this interesting phenomenon happens.

First, as in the proof of Proposition
3.42
, take
\varepsilon_{2}>0
so that inverse map

\text{inv}:f(\beta,\phi)\mapsto\frac{1}{f(\beta,\phi)}

is analytic on
B_{W_{0,N}}\left(-2\mu,\varepsilon_{2}\right)
. Note that

\displaystyle\frac{(\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}+1)\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}}{\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}\overline{\psi}}

\displaystyle{=}\frac{(\beta\partial_{\varphi}+2\mu)\left(\beta\partial_{\beta}+(1-2\mu)\right)\overline{\psi}}{\left(\beta\partial_{\varphi}+(2\mu-1))\overline{\psi}\right)}

\displaystyle=\frac{\beta\partial_{\varphi}(\beta\partial_{\beta}+1)\overline{\psi}-2\mu\beta\partial_{\varphi}\overline{\psi}+2\mu\beta\partial_{\beta}\overline{\psi}+2\mu(1-2\mu)\overline{\psi}}{\left(\beta\partial_{\varphi}+(2\mu-1))\overline{\psi}\right)}

\displaystyle=\frac{\beta\partial_{\varphi}(\beta\partial_{\beta}+1)\overline{\psi}+2\mu\beta\partial_{\beta}\overline{\psi}-2\mu\left(\beta\partial_{\varphi}+(2\mu-1)\right)\overline{\psi}}{\left(\beta\partial_{\varphi}+(2\mu-1))\overline{\psi}\right)}

\displaystyle=\frac{\beta\partial_{\varphi}(\beta\partial_{\beta}+1)\overline{\psi}}{\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}\overline{\psi}}+2\mu\frac{\beta\partial_{\beta}\overline{\psi}}{\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}\overline{\psi}}-2\mu

As

\beta\partial_{\varphi}(\beta\partial_{\beta}+1):X_{0,N}\rightarrow W_{-,N},\qquad\beta\partial_{\beta}:X_{0,N}\rightarrow W_{-,N}

is
C^{1}
by Proposition
3.40
and

\frac{1}{\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}}:B_{X_{0,N}}\left(\overline{\psi}_{0},\delta^{\prime}_{2}\right)\rightarrow W_{+,N}

defined as in Proposition
3.43
is
C^{1}
,

\frac{\beta\partial_{\varphi}(\beta\partial_{\beta}+1)}{\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}}:B_{X_{0,N}}\left(\overline{\psi}_{0},\delta^{\prime}_{2}\right)\underset{\eqref{pm embedding}}{\rightarrow}W_{-,N}\hookrightarrow W_{0,N},\qquad\frac{\beta\partial_{\beta}}{\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}}:B_{X_{0,N}}\left(\overline{\psi}_{0},\delta^{\prime}_{2}\right)\underset{\eqref{pm embedding}}{\rightarrow}W_{-,N}\hookrightarrow W_{0,N}

defined by
