\frac{\beta\partial_{\varphi}(\beta\partial_{\beta}+1)}{\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}}\left(\overline{\psi}\right)=\frac{\beta\partial_{\varphi}(\beta\partial_{\beta}+1)\overline{\psi}}{\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}\overline{\psi}},\qquad\frac{\beta\partial_{\beta}}{\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}}\left(\overline{\psi}\right)=\frac{\beta\partial_{\beta}\overline{\psi}}{\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}\overline{\psi}}

is
C^{1}
by Proposition
3.18
. Since the constant function
2\mu
is an element of
W_{0,N}
,

\frac{(\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}+1)\overline{\partial}_{\beta}}{\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}}:B_{X_{0,N}}\left(\overline{\psi}_{0},\delta^{\prime}_{2}\right)\rightarrow W_{0,N}

is
C^{1}
as the sum of
C^{1}
function. If we take
\delta^{\prime}_{3}<\text{min(}\delta^{\prime}_{2},\delta_{3}
) so that

\frac{(\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}+1)\overline{\partial}_{\beta}}{\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}}\left(B_{X_{0,N}}\left(\overline{\psi}_{0},\delta^{\prime}_{3}\right)\right)\subset B_{W_{0,N}}\left(-2\mu,\varepsilon_{2}\right),

then the map

\frac{\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}}{(\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}+1)\overline{\partial}_{\beta}}\coloneqq\text{inv}\circ\frac{(\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}+1)\overline{\partial}_{\beta}}{\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}}:B_{X_{0,N}}\left(\overline{\psi}_{0},\delta^{\prime}_{3}\right)\rightarrow W_{0,N}

is
C^{1}
.
∎

Proposition 3.45
.

There exists
\delta>0
such that

R:B_{X_{0,N}}\left(\overline{\psi}_{0},\delta\right)\rightarrow W_{0,N},\qquad S:B_{X_{0,N}}\left(\overline{\psi}_{0},\delta\right)\rightarrow W_{+,N}

defined by

\displaystyle R\left(\overline{\psi}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{2\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}\cdot\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}\overline{\psi}}{\left(\bar{\partial}_{\varphi}+1\right)\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}}\left(1+\left(\frac{\partial_{\phi}\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}}{2\overline{\partial}_{\beta}}\right)^{2}\right)-\frac{\partial_{\phi}\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}\cdot\partial_{\phi}\overline{\psi}}{2\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}},

\displaystyle S\left(\overline{\psi}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\left(\bar{\partial}_{\varphi}+1\right)\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}\cdot\partial_{\phi}\overline{\psi}-\partial_{\phi}\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}\cdot\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}\overline{\psi}}{2\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}}

is
C^{1}

Proof.

Use continuity results on each differential operator (Proposition
3.40
,
3.41
,
3.42
,
3.44
) and the fact that product of
C^{1}
map is
C^{1}
by Proposition
3.18
. The constant
\delta
could be chosen
\delta=\text{min}(\delta^{\prime}_{1},\delta^{\prime}_{2},\delta^{\prime}_{3},\delta_{4},\delta_{5})
where
\delta^{\prime}_{1},\delta^{\prime}_{2},\delta^{\prime}_{3}
are the one in Proposition
3.42
,
3.43
,
3.44
and
\delta_{4},\delta_{5}
as in (
3.70
), (
3.71
).
∎

Note that Proposition
3.45
gives
C^{1}
continuity of the ones inside the differential operator
\overline{\partial}_{\varphi},\partial_{\phi}
of
\overline{L}\left(\overline{\psi},\Omega\right)
. To complete the answer for
C^{1}
continuity of
\overline{L}
, we need to show that
\overline{\partial}_{\varphi},\partial_{\phi}
are also
C^{1}
. But what is the range space for
\overline{\partial}_{\varphi},\partial_{\phi}
? To make
P^{(n)}_{+}P^{(n)}_{-}(Q+1)
isometric isomorphism, the answer should be

Z_{0,N}\coloneqq\mathcal{A}^{0.5}\left(Z^{(n)}_{0,N}\right),

where
Z^{(n)}_{0,N}
is as in Definition
3.22
. And then, the way we use to show continuity of

\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}:W_{0,N}\rightarrow Z_{0,N},\qquad\partial_{\phi}:W_{+,N}\rightarrow Z_{0,N}

is almost same as before; decompose the operator into each Fourier mode and show uniform boundedness of it. However, before going further, we verify
Z^{(n)}_{0,N}=P^{(n)}_{+}W^{(n)}_{0,N}
is
\left\langle n\right\rangle^{q}
- uniformly embedded in Banach space so that we can actually define the space
\mathcal{A}^{0.5}\left(Z^{(n)}_{0,N}\right)
.

Proposition 3.46
.

Z^{(n)}_{0,N}=P^{(n)}_{+}W^{(n)}_{0,N}
is
\left\langle n\right\rangle^{1}
- uniformly embedded in

(Q+1)C_{b}^{\delta}+\beta C_{b}^{\delta}+C_{b}^{\delta}+\mathbb{C}+\mathbb{C}\xi_{0}\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\left(\overline{\mathbb{R}}_{+}\right)

Proof.

First, suppose
n\neq 0
so that
W^{(n)}_{0,N}=C_{b}^{\delta}\oplus\mathbb{C}\xi_{0}
. For
f\in C_{b}^{\delta}
,

\displaystyle\left\|P^{(n)}_{+}f\right\|_{(Q+1)C_{b}^{\delta}+\beta C_{b}^{\delta}+C_{b}^{\delta}+\mathbb{C}+\mathbb{C}\xi_{0}}

(3.72)

\displaystyle\underset{\eqref{Pnp}}{}

\displaystyle{\displaystyle=}\left\|\left(\beta(\partial_{\beta}-in)-\left((2+n)\mu-1\right)\right)f\right\|_{(Q+1)C_{b}^{\delta}+\beta C_{b}^{\delta}+C_{b}^{\delta}+\mathbb{C}+\mathbb{C}\xi_{0}}

\displaystyle=\Big\|\underbrace{(Q+1)f}_{\in(Q+1)C_{b}^{\delta}}-\underbrace{n\cdot i\beta f}_{\beta C_{b}^{\delta}}+\underbrace{\left((2+n)\mu-2\right)f}_{\in C_{b}^{\delta}}\Big\|_{(Q+1)C_{b}^{\delta}+\beta C_{b}^{\delta}+C_{b}^{\delta}+\mathbb{C}+\mathbb{C}\xi_{0}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|f\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}+\left\langle n\right\rangle\left\|f\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}+\left|(2+n)\mu-2\right|\left\|f\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\langle n\right\rangle\left\|f\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}

\displaystyle=\left\langle n\right\rangle\left\|P^{(n)}_{+}f\right\|_{P^{(n)}_{+}W^{(n)}_{0,N}}.

Also,

\displaystyle\left\|P^{(n)}_{+}\xi_{0}\right\|_{(Q+1)C_{b}^{\delta}+\beta C_{b}^{\delta}+C_{b}^{\delta}+\mathbb{C}+\mathbb{C}\xi_{0}}

(3.73)

\displaystyle\leq\Big\|\underbrace{\beta\partial_{\beta}\xi_{0}}_{\in C_{b}^{\delta}}-\underbrace{in\beta\xi_{0}}_{C_{b}^{\delta}}-\underbrace{\left((2+n)\mu-1\right)\xi_{0}}_{\in\mathbb{C}\xi_{0}}\Big\|_{(Q+1)C_{b}^{\delta}+\beta C_{b}^{\delta}+C_{b}^{\delta}+\mathbb{C}+\mathbb{C}\xi_{0}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\beta\partial_{\beta}\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}+\left|n\right|\left\|\beta\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}+\left|(2+n)\mu-1\right|\left\|\xi_{0}\right\|_{\mathbb{C}\xi_{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\langle n\right\rangle\Big(\underbrace{\left\|\beta\partial_{\beta}\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}+\left\|\beta\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}+1}_{\text{constant}}\Big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\langle n\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\left\langle n\right\rangle\left\|P^{(n)}_{+}\xi_{0}\right\|_{P^{(n)}_{+}W^{(n)}_{0,N}}.

Combining (
3.72
) and (
3.73
), we have
Z^{(n)}_{0,N}=P^{(n)}_{+}W^{(n)}_{0,N}
is
\left\langle n\right\rangle^{1}
- uniformly embedded in Banach space
(Q+1)C_{b}^{\delta}+\beta C_{b}^{\delta}+C_{b}^{\delta}+\mathbb{C}+\mathbb{C}\xi_{0}
for
n\neq 0
. The only difference for
W^{(0)}_{0,N}
compared to
W^{(n)}_{0,N}\,\,(n\neq 0)
is
\mathbb{C}
. Since

P^{(0)}_{+}c=\beta\partial_{\beta}c-(2\mu-1)c=\underbrace{-(2\mu-1)c}_{\in\mathbb{C}},

we get the result without any trouble.
∎

Now, we verify uniform boundedness of

id:W^{(n)}_{+,N}\rightarrow Z^{(n)}_{0,N},\qquad P^{(n)}:W^{(n)}_{0,N}\rightarrow Z^{(n)}_{0,N}.

Since
W^{(n)}_{+,N}
contains
C_{b}^{\delta},\mathbb{C}\xi_{0},\mathbb{C}\xi_{\infty}
regardless of
n
, we need the following lemma.

Lemma 3.47
.

\left(P^{(n)}_{+}\right)^{-1}:\mathbb{C}\xi_{\infty,N}\rightarrow C_{b,N}^{\delta}\oplus\delta_{0n}\mathbb{C}\xi_{\infty,N}
is uniformly bounded. furthermore, if
n\neq 0
, we have an exact norm bound

\displaystyle\left\|\left(P^{(n)}_{+}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{[\mathbb{C}\xi_{\infty,N},C_{b,N}^{\delta}]}

(3.74)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\left\langle n\right\rangle}\left(\frac{1.05}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\left(\left\|\partial_{\beta}\xi_{\infty}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}+2\mu\left\|\frac{\xi_{\infty}}{\beta}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}\right)+\left(\frac{\left\langle N\right\rangle\mu}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}+1.05\right)\left\|\frac{\xi_{\infty}}{\beta}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}\right).

Proof.

We first treat
n=0
case. From

P^{(0)}_{+}\xi_{\infty}=\beta\partial_{\beta}\xi_{\infty}-(2\mu-1)\xi_{\infty},

we have

\left(P^{(0)}_{+}\right)^{-1}\xi_{\infty}=\frac{1}{2\mu-1}\Bigg(\underbrace{\left(P^{(0)}_{+}\right)^{-1}(\beta\partial_{\beta}\xi_{\infty})}_{\underset{\text{Prop }\ref{D^{-1} in C_b^delta}}{\in C_{b}^{\delta}}}-\underbrace{\xi_{\infty}}_{\in\mathbb{C}\xi_{\infty}}\Bigg)

with

\left\|\left(P^{(0)}_{+}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{[\mathbb{C}\xi_{\infty},C_{b}^{\delta}\oplus\mathbb{C}\xi_{\infty}]}\leq\frac{1}{\left|2\mu-1\right|}\left(\frac{\left\|\beta\partial_{\beta}\xi_{\infty}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}}{\mathrm{dist}\left([-\delta,\delta],2\mu-1\right)}+1\right).

Now, we deal with
n\in N\mathbb{Z}\backslash\{0\}
case. Unfortunately, above strategy does not work since
in\beta\xi_{\infty}
does not belong to
C_{b}
. The trick is to consider
\frac{\xi_{\infty}}{\beta}
which belongs to
C_{b}^{\delta}
. From

\displaystyle P^{(n)}_{+}\left(\frac{\xi_{\infty}}{\beta}\right)

\displaystyle=\beta\partial_{\beta}\left(\frac{\xi_{\infty}}{\beta}\right)-in\beta\cdot\frac{\xi_{\infty}}{\beta}-\left((2+n)\mu-1\right)\frac{\xi_{\infty}}{\beta}

\displaystyle=\partial_{\beta}\xi_{\infty}-in\xi_{\infty}-(2+n)\mu\frac{\xi_{\infty}}{\beta},

we can represent
\xi_{\infty}
as

\xi_{\infty}=\frac{1}{in}\Bigg(\underbrace{\partial_{\beta}\xi_{\infty}}_{\in C_{b}^{\delta}}-(2+n)\mu\underbrace{\frac{\xi_{\infty}}{\beta}}_{\in C_{b}^{\delta}}-P^{(n)}_{+}\left(\frac{\xi_{\infty}}{\beta}\right)\Bigg).

Then,

\displaystyle\left\|\left(P^{(n)}_{+}\right)^{-1}\xi_{\infty}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}

\displaystyle=\bigg\|\underbrace{\left(P^{(n)}_{+}\right)^{-1}(\partial_{\beta}\xi_{\infty})-(2+n)\mu\left(P^{(n)}_{+}\right)^{-1}\left(\frac{\xi_{\infty}}{\beta}\right)-\frac{\xi_{\infty}}{\beta}}_{\in C_{b}^{\delta}}\bigg\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}

\displaystyle{\leq}\frac{1}{\left|n\right|}\left(\frac{\left\|\partial_{\beta}\xi_{\infty}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}+\left|(2+n)\mu\right|\left\|\frac{\xi_{\infty}}{\beta}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}}{\text{dist}([-\delta,\delta],(2+n)\mu-1)}+\left\|\frac{\xi_{\infty}}{\beta}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}\right)

\displaystyle{\leq}\frac{1}{\left|n\right|}\left(\frac{\left\langle N\right\rangle}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\cdot\frac{1}{\left\langle n\right\rangle}\left(\left\|\partial_{\beta}\xi_{\infty}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}+(2+\left|n\right|)\mu\left\|\frac{\xi_{\infty}}{\beta}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}\right)+\left\|\frac{\xi_{\infty}}{\beta}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\left\langle n\right\rangle}\left(\frac{\left\langle N\right\rangle}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\cdot\frac{1}{\left|n\right|}\left(\left\|\partial_{\beta}\xi_{\infty}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}+(2+\left|n\right|)\mu\left\|\frac{\xi_{\infty}}{\beta}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}\right)+\frac{\left\langle n\right\rangle}{\left|n\right|}\left\|\frac{\xi_{\infty}}{\beta}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\left(\text{Use the fact that for }n\in N\mathbb{Z}\backslash\left\{0\right\},\frac{\left\langle n\right\rangle}{\left|n\right|}\text{ achieves maximum at }n=N\geq 4\text{ with }\frac{\left\langle 4\right\rangle}{4}\leq 1.05\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\left\langle n\right\rangle}\left(\frac{1.05}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\left(\left\|\partial_{\beta}\xi_{\infty}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}+2\mu\left\|\frac{\xi_{\infty}}{\beta}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}\right)+\left(\frac{\left\langle N\right\rangle\mu}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}+1.05\right)\left\|\frac{\xi_{\infty}}{\beta}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}\right).

∎

Proposition 3.48
.

id:W^{(n)}_{+,N}\rightarrow Z^{(n)}_{0,N}
is uniformly bounded. Furthermore, if
n\neq 0
, we have an exact norm bound

\displaystyle\left\|id\right\|_{[W^{(n)}_{+,N},Z^{(n)}_{0,N}]}

(3.75)

\displaystyle\leq\Bigg(\frac{\left\langle N\right\rangle}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\left(\frac{\left\|\beta\partial_{\beta}\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}+\left\langle N\right\rangle\left\|\beta\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}+2\right)+\frac{1.05}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\left(\left\|\partial_{\beta}\xi_{\infty}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}+2\mu\left\|\frac{\xi_{\infty}}{\beta}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}\right)

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{\left\langle N\right\rangle\mu}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}+1.05\right)\left\|\frac{\xi_{\infty}}{\beta}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}\Bigg)\cdot\frac{1}{\left\langle n\right\rangle}.

Proof.

By direct calculation, we have

\displaystyle\left\|id\right\|_{[W^{(n)}_{+,N},Z^{(n)}_{0,N}]}

\displaystyle=\left\|\left(P^{(n)}_{+}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{[W^{(n)}_{+,N},W^{(n)}_{0,N}]}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\left(P^{(n)}_{+}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{[C_{b,N}^{\delta},W^{(n)}_{0,N}]}+\left\|\left(P^{(n)}_{+}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{[\mathbb{C}\xi_{0,N},W^{(n)}_{0,N}]}+\left\|\left(P^{(n)}_{+}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{[\mathbb{C}\xi_{\infty,N},W^{(n)}_{0,N}]}

\displaystyle{\leq}\frac{\left\langle N\right\rangle}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\cdot\frac{1}{\left\langle n\right\rangle}+\frac{\left\langle N\right\rangle}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\left(\frac{\left\|\beta\partial_{\beta}\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}+\left\langle N\right\rangle\left\|\beta\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}+1\right)\cdot\frac{1}{\left\langle n\right\rangle}

\displaystyle\qquad+\left(\frac{1.05}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\left(\left\|\partial_{\beta}\xi_{\infty}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}+2\mu\left\|\frac{\xi_{\infty}}{\beta}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}\right)+\left(\frac{\left\langle N\right\rangle\mu}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}+1.05\right)\left\|\frac{\xi_{\infty}}{\beta}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}\right)\cdot\frac{1}{\left\langle n\right\rangle}

\displaystyle=\Bigg(\frac{\left\langle N\right\rangle}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\left(\frac{\left\|\beta\partial_{\beta}\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}+\left\langle N\right\rangle\left\|\beta\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}+2\right)+\frac{1.05}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\left(\left\|\partial_{\beta}\xi_{\infty}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}+2\mu\left\|\frac{\xi_{\infty}}{\beta}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\left(\frac{\left\langle N\right\rangle\mu}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}+1.05\right)\left\|\frac{\xi_{\infty}}{\beta}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}\Bigg)\cdot\frac{1}{\left\langle n\right\rangle}.

∎

To show continuity of
\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}
from
W_{0,N}
to
Z_{0,N}
, we need uniform boundeness of
P^{(n)}
.

Proposition 3.49
.

P^{(n)}:W^{(n)}_{0,N}\rightarrow Z^{(n)}_{0,N}
is uniformly bounded. Furthermore, if
n\neq 0
, we have an exact norm bound

\displaystyle\left\|P^{(n)}\right\|_{[W^{(n)}_{0,N},Z^{(n)}_{0,N}]}

\displaystyle\leq 1+\Bigg(\frac{\left\langle N\right\rangle}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\left(\frac{\left\|\beta\partial_{\beta}\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}+\left\langle N\right\rangle\left\|\beta\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}+2\right)+\frac{1.05}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\left(\left\|\partial_{\beta}\xi_{\infty}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}+2\mu\left\|\frac{\xi_{\infty}}{\beta}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\left(\frac{\left\langle N\right\rangle\mu}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}+1.05\right)\left\|\frac{\xi_{\infty}}{\beta}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}\Bigg)\cdot\frac{\left|(2+n)\mu-1\right|}{\left\langle n\right\rangle}.

Proof.

We can estimate norm of
P^{(n)}:W^{(n)}_{0,N}\rightarrow Z^{(n)}_{0,N}
by

\displaystyle\left\|P^{(n)}\right\|_{[W^{(n)}_{0,N},Z^{(n)}_{0,N}]}

\displaystyle=\left\|P^{(n)}_{+}+\left((2+n)\mu-1\right)\cdot id\right\|_{[W^{(n)}_{0,N},Z^{(n)}_{0,N}]}

\displaystyle\leq\bigg\|\underbrace{P^{(n)}_{+}}_{\text{isometry}}\bigg\|_{[W^{(n)}_{0,N},Z^{(n)}_{0,N}]}+\left|(2+n)\mu-1\right|\left\|id\right\|_{[W^{(n)}_{0,N},Z^{(n)}_{0,N}]}

\displaystyle=1+\left|(2+n)\mu-1\right|\left\|id\right\|_{[W^{(n)}_{0,N},Z^{(n)}_{0,N}]}.

Hence, it is uniformly bounded by Proposition
3.48
. Also, for
n\neq 0
,

\displaystyle\left\|P^{(n)}\right\|_{[W^{(n)}_{0,N},Z^{(n)}_{0,N}]}

\displaystyle\leq 1+\left|(2+n)\mu-1\right|\left\|id\right\|_{[W^{(n)}_{0,N},Z^{(n)}_{0,N}]}

\displaystyle\leq 1+\Bigg(\frac{\left\langle N\right\rangle}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\left(\frac{\left\|\beta\partial_{\beta}\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}+\left\langle N\right\rangle\left\|\beta\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}+2\right)+\frac{1.05}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\left(\left\|\partial_{\beta}\xi_{\infty}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}+2\mu\left\|\frac{\xi_{\infty}}{\beta}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\left(\frac{\left\langle N\right\rangle\mu}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}+1.05\right)\left\|\frac{\xi_{\infty}}{\beta}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}\Bigg)\cdot\frac{\left|(2+n)\mu-1\right|}{\left\langle n\right\rangle}.

∎

Observing how
\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}
and
\partial_{\phi}
are decomposed into each Fourier mode in (
3.38
) and (
3.39
), the following proposition comes directly from Proposition
3.13
.

Proposition 3.50
.

\displaystyle\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}

\displaystyle:W_{0,N}\rightarrow Z_{0,N},

\displaystyle\partial_{\phi}

\displaystyle:W_{+,N}\rightarrow Z_{0,N}

is
C^{1}
.

Corollary 3.51
.

\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}\circ R+\partial_{\phi}\circ S
is
C^{1}
from
B_{X_{0,N}}\left(\overline{\psi}_{0},\delta\right)
to
Z_{0,N}
where
R,S,\delta
are defined as in Proposition
3.45
.

We almost reach to
C^{1}
continuity of
\overline{L}
. The last thing to deal with is the term

\frac{\left(\bar{\partial}_{\varphi}+1\right)\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}\cdot\left(\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}\overline{\psi}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2\mu}}}{2\mu}\Omega.

As a result of Proposition
3.41
,
3.43
, we already know

\frac{\left(\bar{\partial}_{\varphi}+1\right)\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}\cdot\left(\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}\overline{\psi}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2\mu}}}{2\mu}\in\mathcal{A}^{0.5}\left(W^{(n)}_{+,N}\right)

(3.76)

Then, what is the choice for Banach space for
\Omega
so that

\frac{\left(\bar{\partial}_{\varphi}+1\right)\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}\cdot\left(\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}\overline{\psi}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2\mu}}}{2\mu}\Omega

could be an element of
\mathcal{A}^{0.5}\left(Z^{(n)}_{0,N}\right)
?

Let
\mathbb{C}
be a space of constant function on
\overline{\mathbb{R}}_{+}
, and similar to
W^{(n)}_{\triangle,N}
space, define
\mathbb{C}^{(n)}_{N}
by

\mathbb{C}^{(n)}_{N}=\begin{cases}\mathbb{C}&(n\in N\mathbb{Z})\\
0&(n\notin N\mathbb{Z})\end{cases}

It is obvious that
\mathbb{C}^{(n)}_{N}\overset{\sim}{\hookrightarrow}C_{b}\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\left(\overline{\mathbb{R}}_{+}\right)
and for all
i,j\in\mathbb{Z}
, embedding

W^{(i)}_{+,N}\cdot\mathbb{C}^{(j)}_{N}\overset{\sim}{\hookrightarrow}W^{(i+j)}_{+,N}

(3.77)

holds. then, if we define a Banach space
Y_{0,N}
by

Y_{0,N}\coloneqq\mathcal{A}^{-0.5}\left(\mathbb{C}^{(n)}_{N}\right),

the following proposition holds.

Proposition 3.52
.

W_{+,N}\cdot Y_{0,N}\hookrightarrow Z_{0,N}.

Proof.

By Proposition
3.15
, (
3.77
) implies

\underbrace{\mathcal{A}^{0.5}\left(W^{(n)}_{+,N}\right)}_{W_{+,N}}\cdot\underbrace{\mathcal{A}^{-0.5}\left(\mathbb{C}^{(n)}_{N}\right)}_{Y_{0,N}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{A}^{-0.5}\left(W^{(n)}_{+,N}\right)

(3.78)

Therefore, we have to show

\mathcal{A}^{-0.5}\left(W^{(n)}_{+,N}\right)\hookrightarrow\mathcal{A}^{0.5}\left(Z^{(n)}_{0,N}\right)

(3.79)

to complete the proof. For
f=\left\{f^{(n)}\right\}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\in\mathcal{A}^{-0.5}\left(W^{(n)}_{+,N}\right)
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|f\right\|_{\mathcal{A}^{0.5}\left(Z^{(n)}_{0,N}\right)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\left\langle n\right\rangle^{0.5}\left\|f^{(n)}\right\|_{Z^{(n)}_{0,N}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\left\langle n\right\rangle^{0.5}\left\|id\right\|_{[W^{(n)}_{+,N},Z^{(n)}_{0,N}]}\left\|f^{(n)}\right\|_{W^{(n)}_{+,N}}

\displaystyle=\left\|id\right\|_{[W^{(0)}_{+,N},Z^{(0)}_{0,N}]}\left\|f^{(0)}\right\|_{W^{(0)}_{+,N}}+\sum_{n\in N\mathbb{Z}\backslash\{0\}}\left\langle n\right\rangle^{0.5}\left\|id\right\|_{[W^{(n)}_{+,N},Z^{(n)}_{0,N}]}\left\|f^{(n)}\right\|_{W^{(n)}_{+,N}}.

Using uniform norm bound (
3.75
), i.e.,

\left\|id\right\|_{[W^{(n)}_{+,N},Z^{(n)}_{0,N}]}\leq\frac{M}{\left\langle n\right\rangle}\qquad\text{for}\qquad n\neq 0

we have

\displaystyle\left\|f\right\|_{\mathcal{A}^{0.5}\left(Z^{(n)}_{0,N}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|id\right\|_{[W^{(0)}_{+,N},Z^{(0)}_{0,N}]}\left\|f^{(0)}\right\|_{W^{(0)}_{+,N}}+M\sum_{n\in N\mathbb{Z}\backslash\{0\}}\left\langle n\right\rangle^{0.5}\left\langle n\right\rangle^{-1}\left\|f^{(n)}\right\|_{W^{(n)}_{+,N}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\left\|id\right\|_{[W^{(0)}_{+,N},Z^{(0)}_{0,N}]}+M\right)\sum_{n\in N\mathbb{Z}}\left\langle n\right\rangle^{-0.5}\left\|f^{(n)}\right\|_{W^{(n)}_{+,N}}

\displaystyle=\left(\left\|id\right\|_{[W^{(0)}_{+,N},Z^{(0)}_{0,N}]}+M\right)\left\|f\right\|_{\mathcal{A}^{-0.5}\left(W^{(n)}_{+,N}\right)}.

Combining (
3.78
) and (
3.79
), then we get the result.
∎

As a function

\frac{(\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}+1)\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\cdot\left(\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2\mu}}}{2\mu}:B_{X_{0,N}}\left(\overline{\psi}_{0},\delta^{\prime}_{2}\right)\rightarrow W_{+,N}

defined by

\left(\frac{(\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}+1)\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\cdot\left(\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2\mu}}}{2\mu}\right)\left(\overline{\psi}\right)=\frac{(\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}+1)\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}\cdot\left(\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}\overline{\psi}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2\mu}}}{2\mu}

is
C^{1}
by Proposition
3.41
,
3.43
, Proposition
3.52
tells us

\left(\overline{\psi},\Omega\right)\rightarrow\frac{(\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}+1)\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}\cdot\left(\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}\overline{\psi}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2\mu}}}{2\mu}\Omega

(3.80)

is
C^{1}
map from
X_{0,N}\times Y_{0,N}
to
Z_{0,N}
.

Then, finally, we have

Proposition 3.53
.

There exists
\delta^{\prime}_{3}>0
such that

\overline{L}:B_{X_{0,N}}\left(\overline{\psi}_{0},\delta^{\prime}_{3}\right)\times Y_{0,N}\rightarrow Z_{0,N}

is
C^{1}

Proof.

Corollary
3.51
and (
3.80
).
∎

3.5.
Isomorphism in high periodicity and implicit function theorem

Proposition
3.53
gives positive answer to
Question 1
and
Question 2
. To apply implicit function theorem on
\overline{L}
, we have to show

\frac{\partial\overline{L}}{\partial\overline{\psi}}\left(\overline{\psi}_{0},\Omega_{0}\right)=\frac{1}{2\mu^{2}}\left((\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}+\mu^{2}\partial_{\phi}\partial_{\phi})(\overline{\partial}_{\beta}+2\mu)+(2\mu-1)(\overline{\partial}_{\beta}+\overline{\partial}_{\varphi})\right)

is an isomorphism, and as always our philosophy is that we decompose
\frac{\partial\overline{L}}{\partial\overline{\psi}}\left(\overline{\psi}_{0},\Omega_{0}\right)
into each Fourier mode and verify

\left(\frac{\partial\overline{L}}{\partial\overline{\psi}}\left(\overline{\psi}_{0},\Omega_{0}\right)\right)^{(n)}=\frac{1}{2\mu^{2}}\left(P^{(n)}_{+}P^{(n)}_{-}(Q+1)+(2\mu-1)\left(Q-P^{(n)}\right)\right)

is an isomorphism with uniformly lower and upper bounded norm, i.e., there exists
m,M>0
such that

m\leq\left\|P^{(n)}_{+}P^{(n)}_{-}(Q+1)+(2\mu-1)\left(Q-P^{(n)}\right)\right\|_{[X^{(n)}_{0,N},Z^{(n)}_{0,N}]}\leq M

(3.81)

for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}
. Here, note that by the way of construction of
X^{(n)}_{0,N},Z^{(n)}_{0,N}
space,

P^{(n)}_{+}P^{(n)}_{-}(Q+1):X^{(n)}_{0,N}\rightarrow Z^{(n)}_{0,N}

is an isometry, hence has norm exactly 1. Therefore, to show (
3.81
), it suffices to show that

\Big\|(2\mu-1)\underbrace{\Big(Q-P^{(n)}\Big)}_{in\beta}\Big\|_{[X^{(n)}_{0,N},Z^{(n)}_{0,N}]}\leq\alpha<1

(3.82)

for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}
. However, observe that for
n=0
,
Q=P^{(0)}
so that
Q-P^{(n)}
is a zero map. Therefore, it suffices to show (
3.82
) for
n\in N\mathbb{Z}\backslash\{0\}
and this is why we have estimated norm of differential operators only when
n\in N\mathbb{Z}\backslash\{0\}
. For
n\in N\mathbb{Z}\backslash\left\{0\right\}
,

\displaystyle\left\|(2\mu-1)in\beta\right\|_{[X^{(n)}_{0,N},Z^{(n)}_{0,N}]}

(3.83)

\displaystyle\leq(2\mu-1)\left\|id\right\|_{[W^{(n)}_{+,N},Z^{(n)}_{0,N}]}\left\|in\beta\right\|_{[X^{(n)}_{0,N},W^{(n)}_{+,N}]}

\displaystyle\leq(2\mu-1)\left\|id\right\|_{[W^{(n)}_{+,N},Z^{(n)}_{0,N}]}

\displaystyle\cdot\left(\left\|in\beta\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{[C_{b,N}^{\delta},C_{b,N}^{\delta}\oplus\mathbb{C}\xi_{\infty,N}]}+\left\|in\beta\left(Q+1\right)^{-1}\left(P^{(n)}_{-}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{[\mathbb{C}\xi_{0,N},C_{b,N}^{\delta}\oplus\mathbb{C}\xi_{\infty,N}]}\right)

\displaystyle\underset{\text{Prop }\ref{continuity of id second},\eqref{continuity of inbeta}}{}

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\leq}\frac{2\mu-1}{\left\langle N\right\rangle}\Bigg[\frac{\left\langle N\right\rangle}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\left(\frac{\left\|\beta\partial_{\beta}\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}+\left\langle N\right\rangle\left\|\beta\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}+2\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{1.05}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\left(\left\|\partial_{\beta}\xi_{\infty}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}+2\mu\left\|\frac{\xi_{\infty}}{\beta}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}\right)+\left(\frac{\left\langle N\right\rangle\mu}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}+1.05\right)\left\|\frac{\xi_{\infty}}{\beta}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\cdot\Bigg[\left(\frac{1}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}+\frac{66}{5}+\left(\frac{\left\langle N\right\rangle+1}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}+\frac{66}{5}\right)\textnormal{max}\left\{\left\|\beta^{\delta}\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}},\left\|\beta^{-\delta}\xi_{\infty}\right\|_{C_{b}}\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\Bigg(\left(1+\frac{2}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\right)\left\|\beta\partial_{\beta}\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}+\frac{\left\langle N\right\rangle}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\left\|\beta\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}+\frac{\left\langle N\right\rangle}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\left\|\beta^{2}\partial_{\beta}\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\left\|\beta^{2}\partial^{2}_{\beta}\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}+1\Bigg)+\frac{\left\langle N\right\rangle}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\left\|\beta\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}\Bigg].

Now, it is time to specify
\xi_{\infty}
so that we can calculate
C_{b}^{\delta}
norm of various functions in the last inequality. Let

\eta(\beta)=\begin{cases}Ce^{\frac{1}{\left(\beta-\frac{3}{2}\right)^{2}-\frac{1}{4}}}&(1<\beta<2)\\
0&(\text{otherwise}),\end{cases}

where the constant
C
is chosen so that
\int_{0}^{\infty}\eta(\beta)\,d\beta=1
.
14
14

14

C
is approximately 142.25034 and local maximum of
\eta(\beta)
is approximately 2.60541
. This is nothing but translated standard mollifier. And then, define
\xi_{\infty},\xi_{0}
by

\xi_{\infty}=\int_{0}^{\beta}\eta(s)\,ds,\qquad\xi_{0}=1-\xi_{\infty}.

With this explicit function and the fact that
\frac{1}{6}<\delta<\frac{1}{2}
by (
3.50
), we have

\displaystyle\left\|\beta\partial_{\beta}\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}

\displaystyle\leq 5,

(3.84)

\displaystyle\left\|\beta\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}

\displaystyle\leq 2.83,

\displaystyle\left\|\partial_{\beta}\xi_{\infty}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}

\displaystyle\leq 3.5,

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\xi_{\infty}}{\beta}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}

\displaystyle\leq 1,

\displaystyle\left\|\beta^{2}\partial_{\beta}\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}

\displaystyle\leq 7.5,

\displaystyle\left\|\beta^{2}\partial^{2}_{\beta}\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}

\displaystyle\leq 42,

\displaystyle\textnormal{max}\left\{\left\|\beta^{\delta}\xi_{0}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}},\left\|\beta^{-\delta}\xi_{\infty}\right\|_{C_{b}^{\delta}}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq 1.42.

Substitute (
3.84
) to (
3.83
) gives

\displaystyle\left\|(2\mu-1)\left(Q-P^{(n)}\right)\right\|_{[X^{(n)}_{0,N},Z^{(n)}_{0,N}]}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\mu-1}{\left\langle N\right\rangle}\Bigg[\frac{\left\langle N\right\rangle}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\left(\frac{5+2.83\left\langle N\right\rangle}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}+2\right)+\frac{1.05}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}(3.5+2\mu)+\frac{\left\langle N\right\rangle\mu}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}+1.05\Bigg]\cdot

\displaystyle\qquad\Bigg[\left(\frac{1.42\left\langle N\right\rangle}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}+\frac{2.42}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}+31.95\right)\left(\frac{10.33\left\langle N\right\rangle}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}+\frac{52}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}+6\right)+\frac{2.83\left\langle N\right\rangle}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\eqqcolon K.

To estimate K, we give a restriction

N>2000

(3.85)

This restriction seems radical at the first glance. However, we set this lower bound, because estimation of
K
with an assumption (
3.85
) gives the range of
N
larger than 2000 for
K
to be less than 1. For
N>2000
, we have (remember
\mu>\frac{2}{3}
)

\frac{1}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}<0.00076,\qquad\frac{\left\langle N\right\rangle}{(N-2)\mu+\frac{5}{6}}<\frac{1.00101}{\mu}.

(3.86)

Substitute (
3.86
) to
K
, then we have

K\leq\frac{2\mu-1}{\left\langle N\right\rangle}\left(397+\frac{1090}{\mu}+\frac{1264}{\mu^{2}}+\frac{999}{\mu^{3}}+\frac{42}{\mu^{4}}\right).

Hence, if we choose
N
so that

N>(2\mu-1)\left(397+\frac{1090}{\mu}+\frac{1264}{\mu^{2}}+\frac{999}{\mu^{3}}+\frac{42}{\mu^{4}}\right),

(3.87)

then

\left\|(2\mu-1)\left(Q-P^{(n)}\right)\right\|_{[X^{(n)}_{0,N},Z^{(n)}_{0,N}]}\leq K<1

for all
n\in N\mathbb{Z}\backslash\{0\}
and accordingly

\frac{\partial\overline{L}}{\partial\overline{\psi}}\left(\overline{\psi}_{0},\Omega_{0}\right)\in L\left(X_{0,N},Z_{0,N}\right)

becomes isomorphism. Combining all results in this section, we have:

Theorem 3.54
.

Let
\mu\in(\frac{2}{3},\infty)
be given. If

N>(2\mu-1)\left(397+\frac{1090}{\mu}+\frac{1264}{\mu^{2}}+\frac{999}{\mu^{3}}+\frac{42}{\mu^{4}}\right),

then there exists
\varepsilon_{N}^{*}>0,\delta>0
and
C^{1}
map

G:B_{Y_{0,N}}\left(\Omega_{0},\varepsilon_{N}^{*}\right)\rightarrow B_{X_{0,N}}\left(\overline{\psi}_{0},\delta\right)

so that

\overline{L}\left(G(\Omega),\Omega\right)=0

for all
\Omega\in B_{Y_{0,N}}\left(\Omega_{0},\varepsilon_{N}^{*}\right)
.

Proof.

Proposition
3.53
says that

\overline{L}:B_{X_{0,N}}\left(\overline{\psi}_{0},\delta\right)\times Y_{0,N}\rightarrow Z_{0,N}

is
C^{1}
and condition (
3.87
) implies

\frac{\partial\overline{L}}{\partial\overline{\psi}}\left(\overline{\psi}_{0},\Omega_{0}\right)\in L\left(X_{0,N},Z_{0,N}\right)

is an isomorphism. Then, implicit function theorem yields the result.
∎

Remark 3.55
.

For the meaningful result, we hope
G(\Omega)
is an real function when
\Omega
is real (distribution). If we restrict our Banach space
X_{0,N},Y_{0,N},Z_{0,N}
to real function (or distribution), which is closed subspace of them as a fixed point of conjugate map,
\overline{L}
and
\frac{\partial\overline{L}}{\partial\overline{\psi}}
are still well-defined since differential operators send real distribution to the real one. Applying same argument to this real restriction of
L
then gives the desired result.

4.
Main theorem in original coordinate

4.1.
Regularity of stream function and change of coordinate

So far, we have constructed solution
\overline{\psi}^{(\Omega)}=G(\Omega)
in Theorem
3.54
for given
\Omega\in B_{Y_{0,N}}\left(\Omega_{0},\varepsilon^{*}\right)
which satisfies
\overline{L}\left(\overline{\psi}^{(\Omega)},\Omega\right)=0
. Although
\overline{\psi}^{(\Omega)}
is determined by
\Omega
, we simply write
\overline{\psi}
instead of
\overline{\psi}^{(\Omega)}
for simplicity. The remained thing is to recover physical variable
w(x,t),u(x,t)
and
\psi(x,t)
from
\overline{\psi}(\beta,\phi),\Omega(\phi)
and to verify how the conditions and results on Theorem
1.10
is derived.

First, we eliminate bar notation. Remember,
\psi
and
\overline{\psi}
are related through

\displaystyle\psi_{\beta}

\displaystyle=\beta^{-2\mu}\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi},

\displaystyle\psi_{\varphi}

\displaystyle=\beta^{-2\mu}\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}\overline{\psi},

\displaystyle\psi_{\phi}

\displaystyle=\beta^{1-2\mu}\partial_{\phi}\overline{\psi},

\displaystyle\psi_{\beta\phi}

\displaystyle=\beta^{-2\mu}\partial_{\phi}\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi},

\displaystyle\psi_{\beta\varphi}

\displaystyle=\beta^{-2\mu-1}\left(\bar{\partial}_{\varphi}+1\right)\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}.

Since
\overline{\psi}
satisfies
\left\|\overline{\psi}-\overline{\psi}_{0}\right\|_{X_{0,N}}<\delta<
min
(\delta_{1},\delta_{2},\delta_{3},\delta_{4},\delta_{5})
where
\delta_{1},\delta_{2},\delta_{3},\delta_{4},\delta_{5}
is as in (
3.67
), (
3.68
), (
3.69
), (
3.70
) and (
3.71
), we have boundedness

\displaystyle-\frac{3}{2}\leq\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}\leq-\frac{1}{2},\quad\frac{1}{2}\leq\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}

\displaystyle\overline{\psi}\leq\frac{3}{2},\quad-3\mu\leq(\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}+1)\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}\leq-\mu,

(4.1)

\displaystyle-1\leq\partial_{\phi}\overline{\psi}\leq 1,\quad-1\leq\partial_{\phi}\overline{\partial}_{\beta}

\displaystyle\overline{\psi}\leq 1,\quad\frac{1}{4\mu-2}\leq\overline{\psi}\leq\frac{3}{4\mu-2}

for all
(\beta,\phi)\in\overline{\mathbb{R}}_{+}\times\mathbb{T}
. Hence,

\psi_{\beta}<0,\qquad\psi_{\varphi}>0,\qquad\psi_{\beta\varphi}<0

(4.2)

so that (
2.21
) is well-defined. Also, various assumption we imposed on
\psi(\beta,\phi),a(\beta,\phi)
in Proposition
2.2
and Section
2.3
are now justified. Furthermore, by Proposition
3.40
and Proposition
3.41
,

\overline{\psi},\qquad\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi},\qquad\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}\overline{\psi},\qquad(\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}+1)\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi},\qquad\partial_{\phi}\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}

are all belong to
W_{+,N}=\mathcal{A}^{0.5}\left(C_{b,N}^{\delta}\oplus\mathbb{C}\xi_{0,N}\oplus\mathbb{C}\xi_{\infty,N}\right)
. Through an embedding

\mathcal{A}^{0.5}\left(C_{b,N}^{\delta}\oplus\mathbb{C}\xi_{0,N}\oplus\mathbb{C}\xi_{\infty,N}\right)\hookrightarrow\mathcal{A}^{0.5}\left(C_{b}\right)\hookrightarrow\mathcal{A}^{0}\left(C_{b}\right)\underset{\text{M-test}}{\hookrightarrow}C_{b}\left(\overline{\mathbb{R}}_{+}\times\mathbb{T}\right),

it implies

\psi,\qquad\psi_{\beta},\qquad\psi_{\varphi},\qquad\psi_{\phi},\qquad\psi_{\beta\phi},\qquad\psi_{\beta\varphi}

(4.3)

all belong to
C\left(\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{T}\right)
, where
C\left(\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{T}\right)
is a set of continuous (not need to be bounded) function on
\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{T}
. Now, we can justify special change of coordinate we did in Section
2.2
.

Proposition 4.1
.

A map
T\coloneqq B\circ A:\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{T}\overset{A}{\longrightarrow}\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}\overset{B}{\longrightarrow}\mathbb{R}^{2}\backslash\left\{0\right\}
defined by composition of two maps

(\beta,\phi)\quad\xrightarrow[\begin{cases}a=\frac{1}{2}\log\left(-\frac{\psi_{\beta}}{\mu}\right)\\
\theta=\beta+\phi\end{cases}]{A}\quad(a,\theta)\quad\xrightarrow[\begin{cases}z_{1}=e^{a}\cos\theta\\
z_{2}=e^{a}\sin\theta\end{cases}]{B}\quad(z_{1},z_{2})

is a
C^{1}
-diffeomorphism.

Proof.

It is well known that polar coordinate change map
B
is a
C^{\infty}
-diffeomorphism, so it suffices to prove the result for
A
.

First,

a(\beta,\theta)=\frac{1}{2}\log\left(-\frac{\psi_{\beta}}{\mu}\right)

is well defined since
\psi_{\beta}
is strictly negative by (
4.2
).

Second, we prove surjectivity. Since

\psi_{\beta}=\beta^{-2\mu}\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}

and

\,\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}\in C\left(\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{T}\right)\qquad\text{with}\qquad-\frac{3}{2}\leq\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}\leq-\frac{1}{2},

intermediate value theorem guarantees surjectivity.

Last, we prove injectivity. Suppose

\left(a(\beta_{1},\phi_{1}),\beta_{1}+\phi_{1}\right)=\left(a(\beta_{2},\phi_{2}),\beta_{2}+\phi_{2}\right)=(a_{0},\theta_{0}).

The set of
(\beta,\phi)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{T}
which satisfies
\beta+\phi=\theta_{0}
is represented as Figure
3
.

Figure 3.
A level set of
\beta+\phi

Then, it can be easily seen that
(\beta_{1},\phi_{1})\neq(\beta_{2},\phi_{2})
as in Figure
3
leads to contradiction since directional derivative of
a(\beta,\phi)
to the direction of
(1,-1)
is

D_{(1,-1)}a(\beta,\phi)=\partial_{\beta}a(\beta,\phi)-\partial_{\phi}a(\beta,\phi)=-a_{\varphi}\underset{\eqref{new coordinate}}{=}-\frac{\psi_{\beta\varphi}}{\psi_{\beta}}

which is strictly negative for all
(\beta,\phi)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{T}
. Therefore,
A
is
C^{1}
bijective map. Furthermore, since

J_{A}=\det\begin{pmatrix}a_{\beta}&a_{\phi}\\
1&1\end{pmatrix}=-a_{\varphi}<0,

for all
(\beta,\phi)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{T}
, its inverse is also
C^{1}
by inverse function theorem.
∎

Before doing change of coordinate, we analyze the regularity of vorticity
w(\beta,\phi)
. As we have relation

w(\beta,\phi)=\left(\psi_{\varphi}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2\mu}}\Omega(\phi),

we have to investigate regularity of
\Omega(\phi)
. However, the problem is that
\Omega(\phi)
belongs to
\mathcal{A}^{-0.5}\left(\mathbb{C}^{(n)}_{N}\right)
, from which we cannot guarantee that
\Omega
is continuous, or even a function! Therefore, we impose further restriction on
\Omega(\phi)
. We assume

\Omega\in\mathcal{A}^{-0.5}\left(\mathbb{C}^{(n)}_{N}\right)\cap L^{p}(\mathbb{T})

so that

w(\beta,\phi)\in L^{p}_{\textnormal{loc}}(\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{T})

for some fixed
p\in[1,2\mu)
15
15

15

The reason we require
p\in[1,2\mu)
is to guarantee the local integrability of
w(x,t)
. This will be seen at Proposition
4.11
. Then, we have

\displaystyle\psi(\beta,\phi)\in C^{1}(\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{T}),\quad w(\beta,\phi)\in L^{p}_{\textnormal{loc}}(\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{T})

(4.4)

\displaystyle\underset{\text{Prop }\ref{C^1 change of coordinate}}{}

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\Longrightarrow}\quad\widetilde{\psi}(z)\in C^{1}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}\backslash\left\{0\right\}\right),\,\widetilde{u}(z)\coloneqq\nabla^{\perp}_{z}\widetilde{\psi}(z)\in C\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}\backslash\left\{0\right\}\right),\widetilde{w}(z)\in L^{p}_{\textnormal{loc}}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}\backslash\left\{0\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\underset{\eqref{tilde func}}{}

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\Longrightarrow}\psi(\cdot,t)\in C^{1}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}\backslash\left\{0\right\}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}\right),\,u(\cdot,t)\in C\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}\backslash\left\{0\right\}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}\right),\,w(\cdot,t)\in L^{p}_{\textnormal{loc}}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}\backslash\left\{0\right\}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}\right).

Also, reading the Section
2.3
and
2.4
backwards, we can recover vorticity stream relation

\displaystyle\overline{L}(\overline{\psi}(\beta,\phi),\Omega(\phi))=0

(4.5)

\displaystyle\Rightarrow L\left(\psi(\beta,\phi),\Omega(\phi)\right)=0

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\begin{pmatrix}\partial_{\beta}\\
\partial_{\phi}\\
\end{pmatrix}\cdot\Bigg(\underbrace{\left|J_{T}\right|J_{T}^{-1}}_{\text{adj}(J_{T})}\underbrace{\left(J_{T}^{-1}\right)^{t}\begin{pmatrix}\partial_{\beta}\\
\partial_{\phi}\\
\end{pmatrix}}_{\nabla_{z}}\psi\Bigg)=\left|J_{T}\right|w

\displaystyle\underset{\eqref{adjoint formula}}{}

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\Rightarrow}\nabla_{z}\cdot\left(\nabla_{z}\widetilde{\psi}\right)=\widetilde{w}

in the sense of distribution.

4.2.
Weak solution on
\mathbb{R}^{2}\backslash\{0\}\times[a,\infty)
for
a>0

To show
w(x,t),u(x,t)
is a weak solution of Euler equation, we mollify
w(\beta,\phi)
, due to the lacks of regularity. Let
\eta
be a standard mollifier in one dimension and we write
\Omega^{\varepsilon}(\phi)
to denote
\left(\Omega(\cdot)*\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\eta\left(\frac{\cdot}{\varepsilon}\right)\right)(\phi)
. Then, for
w(\beta,\phi)=\left(\psi_{\varphi}(\beta,\phi)\right)^{-\frac{1}{2\mu}}\Omega(\phi)
, we define
w^{\dot{\varepsilon}}(\beta,\phi)
by

w^{\dot{\varepsilon}}(\beta,\phi)=\left(\psi_{\varphi}(\beta,\phi)\right)^{-\frac{1}{2\mu}}\Omega^{\varepsilon}(\phi)

(4.6)

(note that we only mollify
\Omega
since
\left(\psi_{\varphi}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2\mu}}
is already
C^{1}
). Now we show that
w^{\dot{\varepsilon}}\xrightarrow[\varepsilon\searrow 0]{}w
in
L^{p}_{\textnormal{loc}}(\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{T})
for
p<\infty
.

Proposition 4.2
.

For any
1\leq p<\infty
and
0<a<b<\infty
,

w^{\dot{\varepsilon}}\longrightarrow w

in
L^{p}\left([a,b]\times\mathbb{T}\right)
.

Proof.

\displaystyle\left\|w^{\dot{\varepsilon}}-w\right\|_{L^{p}\left([a,b]\times\mathbb{T}\right)}

\displaystyle=\left\|\left(\psi_{\varphi}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2\mu}}\left(\Omega^{\varepsilon}-\Omega\right)\right\|_{L^{p}\left([a,b]\times\mathbb{T}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\sup_{(\beta,\phi)\in[a,b]\times\mathbb{T}}\left(\psi_{\varphi}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2\mu}}\right)\big\|\underbrace{\Omega^{\varepsilon}-\Omega}_{\begin{subarray}{c}\text{does not}\\
\text{depend on }\beta\end{subarray}}\big\|_{L^{p}\left([a,b]\times\mathbb{T}\right)}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{(b-a)^{\frac{1}{p}}\left(\sup_{(\beta,\phi)\in[a,b]\times\mathbb{T}}\left(\psi_{\varphi}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2\mu}}\right)}_{<\infty}\left\|\Omega^{\varepsilon}-\Omega\right\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T})}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow[\varepsilon\searrow 0]{}0

by a property of standard mollifier.
∎

Remark 4.3
.

If
p=\infty
, by the inclusion
L^{q}(\mathbb{T})\subset L^{r}(\mathbb{T})
for all
1\leq r\leq q\leq\infty
, the convergence holds for all
1\leq p<\infty
. In particular,
w^{\dot{\varepsilon}}\rightarrow w
in
L^{1}([a,b]\times\mathbb{T})
regardless of
p\geq 1
.

Since
w^{\dot{\varepsilon}}(\beta,\phi)
is
C^{1}
and
\left(\psi_{\varphi}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\mu}}w^{\dot{\varepsilon}}
depends only on
\phi
, reading (
2.17
) backwards gives

-\widetilde{w}^{\dot{\varepsilon}}(z)+\left(\nabla^{\perp}_{z}\widetilde{\psi}(z)-\mu z\right)\cdot\nabla_{z}\widetilde{w}^{\dot{\varepsilon}}(z)=0.

(4.7)

Using (
4.7
), we can verify the first equation of (
2.4
).

Proposition 4.4
.

(-1+2\mu)\widetilde{w}+\nabla_{z}\cdot\left(\widetilde{w}\left(\nabla^{\perp}_{z}\widetilde{\psi}-\mu z\right)\right)=0

on
\mathbb{R}^{2}\backslash\left\{0\right\}
in weak sense, i.e., for any test function
\widetilde{f}\in C^{\infty}_{c}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}\backslash\left\{0\right\}\right)
,

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}\backslash\left\{0\right\}}(-1+2\mu)\widetilde{w}\widetilde{f}-\widetilde{w}\left(\left(\nabla^{\perp}_{z}\widetilde{\psi}-\mu z\right)\cdot\nabla_{z}\widetilde{f}\right)=0.

Proof.

For any test function
\widetilde{f}\in C^{\infty}_{c}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}\backslash\left\{0\right\}\right)
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}\backslash\{0\}}\widetilde{w}^{\dot{\varepsilon}}\widetilde{f}+\widetilde{w}^{\dot{\varepsilon}}\left(\left(\nabla^{\perp}_{z}\widetilde{\psi}-\mu z\right)\cdot\nabla_{z}\widetilde{f}\right)

\displaystyle{=}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}\backslash\{0\}}\left(\left(\nabla^{\perp}_{z}\widetilde{\psi}-\mu z\right)\cdot\nabla_{z}\widetilde{w}^{\dot{\varepsilon}}\right)\widetilde{f}+\widetilde{w}^{\dot{\varepsilon}}\left(\nabla^{\perp}_{z}\widetilde{\psi}-\mu z\right)\cdot\nabla_{z}\widetilde{f}

\displaystyle{=}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}\backslash\{0\}}\left(\nabla^{\perp}_{z}\widetilde{\psi}-\mu z\right)\cdot\nabla_{z}\left(\widetilde{w}^{\dot{\varepsilon}}\widetilde{f}\right)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}\backslash\{0\}}\underbrace{\nabla^{\perp}_{z}\widetilde{\psi}\cdot\nabla_{z}\left(\widetilde{w}^{\dot{\varepsilon}}\widetilde{f}\right)}_{\begin{subarray}{c}=0\,(\because\,\nabla^{\perp}_{z}\psi\text{ is weakly}\\
\text{divergence free)}\end{subarray}}-\mu z\cdot\nabla_{z}\left(\widetilde{w}^{\dot{\varepsilon}}\widetilde{f}\right)

\displaystyle{=}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}\backslash\{0\}}\left(\mu\nabla_{z}\cdot z\right)\widetilde{w}^{\dot{\varepsilon}}\widetilde{f}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}\backslash\{0\}}2\mu\widetilde{w}^{\dot{\varepsilon}}\widetilde{f}.

Therefore, we have

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}\backslash\left\{0\right\}}(-1+2\mu)\widetilde{w}^{\dot{\varepsilon}}\widetilde{f}-\widetilde{w}^{\dot{\varepsilon}}\left(\left(\nabla^{\perp}_{z}\widetilde{\psi}-\mu z\right)\cdot\nabla_{z}\widetilde{f}\right)=0

(4.8)

for all
\varepsilon>0
. Let
\text{supp}\widetilde{f}=K
and take
0<a<b<\infty
such that
T^{-1}(K)\subset[a,b]\times\mathbb{T}
. Then,

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}\backslash\{0\}}\left(\widetilde{w}^{\dot{\varepsilon}}\widetilde{f}-\widetilde{w}\widetilde{f}\right)\,dz\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\int_{T\left([a,b]\times\mathbb{T}\right)}\left(\widetilde{w}^{\dot{\varepsilon}}\widetilde{f}-\widetilde{w}\widetilde{f}\right)\,dz\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\int_{[a,b]\times\mathbb{T}}\left(w^{\dot{\varepsilon}}f-wf\right)\left|\det J_{T}\right|\,d\beta d\phi\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|w^{\dot{\varepsilon}}-w\right\|_{L^{1}\left([a,b]\times\mathbb{T}\right)}\underbrace{\left\|f\det J_{T}\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left([a,b]\times\mathbb{T}\right)}}_{<\infty\,(\because\,f,J_{T}\text{ is continuous})}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow[\varepsilon\searrow 0]{\text{Prop }\ref{L^p convergence of w}}0.

In similar way, we have

\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}\backslash\{0\}}\left(\widetilde{w}^{\dot{\varepsilon}}-\widetilde{w}\right)\left(\left(\nabla^{\perp}_{z}\widetilde{\psi}-\mu z\right)\cdot\nabla_{z}\widetilde{f}\right)\right|\xrightarrow[\varepsilon\searrow 0]{}0.

Therefore, sending
\varepsilon
to 0 in (
4.8
) gives desired result.
∎

Having proved
\widetilde{w}(z)
is a weak solution for (
2.4
), we now prove vorticity in space-time coordinate, i.e.,
w(x,t)=t^{-1}\widetilde{w}\left(xt^{-\mu}\right)
, is a weak solution of (
2.1
). Let
w^{\dot{\varepsilon}}(x,t)\coloneqq t^{-1}\widetilde{w}^{\dot{\varepsilon}}\left(xt^{-\mu}\right)
and remember
u(x,t)=t^{\mu-1}\nabla^{\perp}_{z}\widetilde{\psi}\left(xt^{-\mu}\right)
.

Lemma 4.5
.

For all
\varepsilon>0
,

w^{\dot{\varepsilon}}_{t}+\nabla_{x}\cdot\left(w^{\dot{\varepsilon}}u\right)=0

on
\mathbb{R}^{2}\backslash\{0\}\times[a,\infty)
in distribution sense for any
a>0
.

Proof.

Remember that

-\widetilde{w}^{\dot{\varepsilon}}(z)+\left(\nabla^{\perp}_{z}\widetilde{\psi}(z)-\mu z\right)\cdot\nabla_{z}\widetilde{w}^{\dot{\varepsilon}}(z)=0

in classical sense and
\widetilde{w}^{\dot{\varepsilon}}(z)
is
C^{1}
. Then, we can trace back from (
2.4
) to (
2.1
), which leads to

w^{\dot{\varepsilon}}_{t}+u\cdot\nabla_{x}w^{\dot{\varepsilon}}=0

(4.9)

in classical sense on
\mathbb{R}^{2}\backslash\{0\}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}
. Then, for a test function
f\in C^{\infty}_{c}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}\backslash\{0\}\right)
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}\backslash\left\{0\right\}}w(\cdot,t)f(\cdot,t)\,dx+\int_{a}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}\backslash\left\{0\right\}}w^{\dot{\varepsilon}}f_{t}+w^{\dot{\varepsilon}}(u\cdot\nabla_{x}f)\,dxdt

\displaystyle{=}-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}\backslash\{0\}\times[a,\infty)}(w^{\dot{\varepsilon}}f)_{t}\,dxdt+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}\backslash\{0\}\times[a,\infty)}w^{\dot{\varepsilon}}f_{t}-f(u\cdot\nabla_{x}w^{\dot{\varepsilon}})+u\cdot\nabla_{x}(w^{\dot{\varepsilon}}f)\,dxdt

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}\backslash\{0\}\times[a,\infty)}-\underbrace{\left(w^{\dot{\varepsilon}}_{t}+u\cdot\nabla_{x}w^{\dot{\varepsilon}}\right)}_{=0\text{ by}\eqref{eq49}}f+\underbrace{u\cdot\nabla_{x}\left(w^{\dot{\varepsilon}}f\right)}_{\begin{subarray}{c}=0\,(\because\,u\text{ is weakly }\\
\text{divergence free})\end{subarray}}\,dxdt.

\displaystyle=0

∎

Proposition 4.6
.

The
(w(x,t),u(x,t),\psi(x,t))
given in (
4.4
) satisfies

\left\{\begin{aligned} &w_{t}+\nabla_{x}\cdot(wu)=0&on\quad\mathbb{R}^{2}\backslash\{0\}\times[a,\infty),\\
&u=\nabla_{x}^{\perp}\psi,\quad w=\Delta_{x}\psi&on\quad\mathbb{R}^{2}\backslash\{0\}\end{aligned}\right.

in distribution sense for any
a>0
.

Proof.

First,
u=\nabla^{\perp}_{x}\psi
is clear by the definition of
u(x,t)
in (
2.3
) and (
4.4
). Also,
\Delta_{x}\psi=w
could be obtained directly from (
4.5
).

To prove the first equation, take
f\in C^{\infty}_{c}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}\backslash\{0\}\times[a,\infty)\right)
. Then, we can take annulus
K=\overline{B(r,R)}\coloneqq\left\{z\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\,|\,r\leq\left|z\right|\leq R\right\}
and
0<a<b<\infty
such that

\text{supp}f\in\overline{B(r,R)}\times[a,b].

Let
Q\coloneqq\overline{B\left(\frac{r}{b^{\mu}},\frac{R}{a^{\mu}}\right)}
. By Proposition
4.2
and Remark
4.3
, we have

\widetilde{w}^{\dot{\varepsilon}}(z)\rightarrow\widetilde{w}(z)

in
L^{1}(Q)
. Then,

\displaystyle\int_{K\times[a,b]}\left|w^{\dot{\varepsilon}}(x,t)-w(x,t)\right|\,dxdt

\displaystyle{=}\int_{a}^{b}\int_{K}t^{-1}\left|\widetilde{w}^{\dot{\varepsilon}}(xt^{-\mu})-\widetilde{w}(xt^{-\mu})\right|\,dxdt

\displaystyle{=}\int_{a}^{b}\int_{t^{-\mu}K}t^{-1}\left|\widetilde{w}^{\dot{\varepsilon}}(z)-\widetilde{w}(z)\right|t^{2\mu}\,dzdt

\displaystyle{\leq}\int_{a}^{b}t^{2\mu-1}\int_{Q}\left|\widetilde{w}^{\dot{\varepsilon}}(z)-\widetilde{w}(z)\right|\,dzdt

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2\mu}\left(b^{2\mu}-a^{2\mu}\right)\cdot\underbrace{\int_{Q}\left|\widetilde{w}^{\dot{\varepsilon}}(z)-\widetilde{w}(z)\right|\,dz}_{\xrightarrow[\varepsilon\searrow 0]{}0\text{ by Prop }\ref{L^p convergence of w}},

which means

w^{\dot{\varepsilon}}(x,t)\xrightarrow[\varepsilon\searrow 0]{}w(x,t)

(4.10)

in
L^{1}\left(K\times[a,b]\right)
. Then, we have

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle{=}-\int_{K}w^{\dot{\varepsilon}}(\cdot,a)f(\cdot,a)-\int_{K\times[a,b]}w^{\dot{\varepsilon}}(f_{t}+u\cdot\nabla f)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow[\varepsilon\searrow 0]{}-\int_{K}w(\cdot,a)f(\cdot,a)-\int_{K\times[a,b]}w(f_{t}+u\cdot\nabla f),

because

\displaystyle\left|\int_{K}\left(w^{\dot{\varepsilon}}(x,a)-w(x,a)\right)f(x,a)\,dx\right|=\left|\int_{K}\left(\widetilde{w}^{\dot{\varepsilon}}(xa^{-\mu})-\widetilde{w}(xa^{-\mu})\right)f(x,a)\,dx\right|

\displaystyle{=}\left|\int_{a^{-\mu}K}\left(\widetilde{w}^{\dot{\varepsilon}}(z)-\widetilde{w}(z)\right)f(a^{\mu}z,a)a^{2\mu}\,dz\right|\underset{a^{-\mu}K\subset Q}{\leq}\left|\int_{Q}\left(\widetilde{w}^{\dot{\varepsilon}}(z)-\widetilde{w}(z)\right)f(a^{\mu}z,a)a^{2\mu}\,dz\right|

\displaystyle\leq\underbrace{\left\|\widetilde{w}^{\dot{\varepsilon}}(z)-\widetilde{w}(z)\right\|_{L^{1}(Q)}}_{\xrightarrow[\varepsilon\searrow 0]{}0\,(\because\,\text{Prop }\ref{L^p convergence of w})}\underbrace{\left\|a^{2\mu}f(a^{\mu}z,a)\right\|_{L^{\infty}(Q)}}_{<\infty\,(\because\,f\text{ is continuous)}}

and

\left|\int_{K\times[a,b]}(w^{\dot{\varepsilon}}-w)(f_{t}+u\cdot\nabla f)\right|\leq\underbrace{\left\|w^{\dot{\varepsilon}}-w\right\|_{L^{1}(K\times[a,b])}}_{\xrightarrow[\varepsilon\searrow 0]{}0}\underbrace{\left\|f_{t}+u\cdot\nabla f\right\|_{L^{\infty}(K\times[a,b])}}_{\begin{subarray}{c}<\infty\,(\because\,u\text{ and }f\\
\text{is continuous}\end{subarray}}

∎

Having treated Euler equation in vorticity form, we now deal with velocity form. First, we show the following lemma.

Lemma 4.7
.

\nabla_{x}\cdot(wu)=\nabla^{\perp}_{x}\cdot\left(\nabla_{x}\cdot(u\otimes u)\right)

on
\mathbb{R}^{2}\backslash\{0\}
in the sense of distribution.

Proof.

Take a test function
h\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{2}\backslash\{0\})
. What we have to show is

-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}\backslash\{0\}}w(u\cdot\nabla h)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}\backslash\{0\}}(u\otimes u)\,:\,\nabla\nabla^{\perp}h.

Let
\text{supp}(h)=K
,
U=\bigcup_{x\in K}B\left(x,\frac{\mathrm{dist}(K,\left\{0\right\}}{4}\right)
and
\eta(x)
be a standard mollifier in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
. We define

w^{\varepsilon}(x,t)\coloneqq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}w(x-y,t)\cdot\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\eta\left(\frac{y}{\varepsilon}\right)\,dy

for
x\in U,\varepsilon<\frac{\mathrm{dist}(K,\left\{0\right\})}{4}
, and
u^{\varepsilon}(x,t),\psi^{\varepsilon}(x,t)
accordingly. From the compatibility of mollifier with differential operator and the fact that

\Delta_{x}\psi=w,\qquad\nabla_{x}\cdot u=0,\qquad\nabla^{\perp}_{x}\psi=u,\qquad\nabla^{\perp}_{x}\cdot u=w

in distribution sense, we have

\Delta_{x}\psi^{\varepsilon}=w^{\varepsilon},\qquad\nabla_{x}\cdot u^{\varepsilon}=0,\qquad\nabla^{\perp}_{x}\psi^{\varepsilon}=u^{\varepsilon},\qquad\nabla^{\perp}_{x}\cdot u^{\varepsilon}=w^{\varepsilon}

in classical sense on
U
. Then, it is clear that

\nabla_{x}\cdot(w^{\varepsilon}u^{\varepsilon})=\nabla^{\perp}_{x}\cdot\left(\nabla_{x}\cdot(u^{\varepsilon}\otimes u^{\varepsilon})\right),

and this implies

-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}\backslash\{0\}}w^{\varepsilon}(u^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla h)=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}\backslash\{0\}}(u^{\varepsilon}\otimes u^{\varepsilon})\,:\,\nabla\nabla^{\perp}h.

(4.11)

Since
w^{\varepsilon}\xrightarrow[\varepsilon\searrow 0]{}w
in
L^{1}(K)
and
u^{\varepsilon}\xrightarrow[\varepsilon\searrow 0]{}u
uniformly on K, sending
\varepsilon
to 0 in (
4.11
) yields the result.
∎

Proposition 4.8
.

\nabla^{\perp}_{x}\cdot\left(u_{t}+\nabla_{x}\cdot(u\otimes u)\right)=0

on
\mathbb{R}^{2}\backslash\{0\}\times[a,\infty)
in the sense of distribution for any
a>0
.

Proof.

Proposition
4.6
and Lemma
4.7
.
∎

4.3.
Convergence to initial data and time independent boundedness

Note that Proposition
4.6
tells us that constructed
w(x,t)
is a weak solution of Euler equation on time
(0,\infty)
, not on
[0,\infty)
. To show
w
and
u
is a weak solution on time
[0,\infty)
, we have to investigate the initial data corresponding to
(w(x,t),u(x,t),\psi(x,t))
.

Proposition 4.9
.

The stream function
\psi(x,t)
recovered from
\overline{\psi}(\beta,\phi)\underset{\text{Thm }\ref{main thm in special coordinate}}{=}G(\Omega)
through (
4.4
) satisfies

\lim_{t\searrow 0}\psi(x,t)=\left|x\right|^{2-\frac{1}{\mu}}\left(-\frac{\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}(0,\theta)}{\mu}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\mu}-1}\overline{\psi}(0,\theta)

(4.12)

locally uniformly on
\mathbb{R}^{2}\backslash\{0\}
.

Proof.

We have

\psi(x,t)\underset{\eqref{tilde func}}{=}t^{2\mu-1}\widetilde{\psi}(xt^{-\mu})\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}(\beta,\phi)=T^{-1}(xt^{-\mu})\\
\psi(\beta,\phi)=\beta^{1-2\mu}\overline{\psi}(\beta,\phi)\end{subarray}}{=}\left(\frac{\beta}{t}\right)^{1-2\mu}\overline{\psi}(\beta,\phi).

(4.13)

Also,

\left|x\right|=t^{\mu}\left|z\right|\underset{\eqref{log-polar coordinate}}{=}t^{\mu}e^{a(\beta,\phi)}\underset{\eqref{new coordinate}}{=}t^{\mu}\left(-\frac{\psi_{\beta}}{\mu}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\underset{\eqref{beta and bar beta}}{=}\left(\frac{t}{\beta}\right)^{\mu}\left(-\frac{\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}}{\mu}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(4.14)

From (
4.14
), we have

\frac{\beta}{t}=\left|x\right|^{-\frac{1}{\mu}}\left(-\frac{\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}(\beta,\phi)}{\mu}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\mu}},

(4.15)

and substituting (
4.15
) to (
4.13
) gives

\psi(x,t)=\left|x\right|^{2-\frac{1}{\mu}}\left(-\frac{\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}(\beta,\phi)}{\mu}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\mu}-1}\overline{\psi}(\beta,\phi),

(4.16)

where
(\beta,\phi)=T^{-1}(xt^{-\mu})
. Therefore, we have to investigate
\lim_{t\searrow 0}T^{-1}(xt^{-\mu})
. By using boundedness of
\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}
as in (
4.1
), we get from (
4.15
) that

\beta\leq\left|x\right|^{-\frac{1}{\mu}}\left(\frac{3}{2\mu}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\mu}}t.

(4.17)

Therefore, for all compact region
R
on
\mathbb{R}^{2}\backslash\left\{0\right\}
,
\beta\rightarrow 0
as
t\rightarrow 0
for uniform rate
0\leq\beta<C_{R}t
.
17
17

17

Explicitly,
C_{R}=\left(\frac{3}{2\mu}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\mu}}\cdot\mathrm{dist}(0,R)^{-\frac{1}{\mu}}
.
From this relation of
\beta
and
t
, we have for
x\in R
,

\left|T^{-1}(xt^{-\mu})-(0,\theta)\right|=\left|(\beta,\phi)-(0,\theta)\right|\underset{\theta=\beta+\phi}{=}\sqrt{2}\beta<\sqrt{2}C_{R}t,

i.e.,
(\beta,\phi)
in (
4.16
) converges uniformly to
(0,\theta)
as times goes to zero for any compact set
R\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\backslash\left\{0\right\}
. Since

\left(-\frac{\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}(\beta,\phi)}{\mu}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\mu}-1}\overline{\psi}(\beta,\phi)

is uniformly continuous on compact subset of
\overline{\mathbb{R}}_{+}\times\mathbb{T}
,
\psi(x,t)
in (
4.16
) converges uniformly to

\left|x\right|^{2-\frac{1}{\mu}}\left(-\frac{\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}(0,\theta)}{\mu}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\mu}-1}\overline{\psi}(0,\theta).

∎

Next, we investigate the initial velocity.

Proposition 4.10
.

The velocity field
u(x,t)
satisfies

\displaystyle\lim_{t\searrow 0}u(x,t)

\displaystyle=\left|x\right|^{1-\frac{1}{\mu}}\left(-\frac{\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}(0,\theta)}{\mu}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\mu}-1}\frac{2\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}(0,\theta)}{(\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}+1)\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}(0,\theta)}

(4.18)

\displaystyle\cdot\left(\frac{\partial_{\phi}\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}(0,\theta)\cdot\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}\overline{\psi}(0,\theta)-(\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}+1)\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}(0,\theta)\cdot\partial_{\phi}\overline{\psi}(0,\theta)}{2\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}(0,\theta)}\begin{pmatrix}\cos\theta\\
\sin\theta\end{pmatrix}+\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}\overline{\psi}(0,\theta)\begin{pmatrix}-\sin\theta\\
\cos\theta\end{pmatrix}\right)

locally uniformly on
\mathbb{R}^{2}\backslash\left\{0\right\}
.

Proof.

\displaystyle u(x,t)

\displaystyle{=}t^{\mu-1}\nabla^{\perp}_{z}\widetilde{\psi}(xt^{-\mu})

\displaystyle{=}\frac{t^{\mu-1}}{e^{a}a_{\varphi}}\begin{pmatrix}-a_{\phi}\cos\theta+\sin\theta&a_{\beta}\cos\theta-\sin\theta\\
-a_{\phi}\sin\theta-\cos\theta&a_{\beta}\sin\theta+\cos\theta\\
\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\partial_{\beta}\\
\partial_{\phi}\\
\end{pmatrix}\psi

\displaystyle=\frac{t^{\mu-1}}{e^{a}a_{\varphi}}\left((-a_{\phi}\psi_{\beta}+a_{\beta}\psi_{\phi})\begin{pmatrix}\cos\theta\\
\sin\theta\\
\end{pmatrix}+\psi_{\varphi}\begin{pmatrix}-\sin\theta\\
\cos\theta\\
\end{pmatrix}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{t^{\mu-1}}{e^{a}a_{\varphi}}\left((-a_{\phi}(\psi_{\phi}-\psi_{\varphi})+(a_{\phi}-a_{\varphi})\psi_{\phi})\begin{pmatrix}\cos\theta\\
\sin\theta\\
\end{pmatrix}+\psi_{\varphi}\begin{pmatrix}-\sin\theta\\
\cos\theta\\
\end{pmatrix}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{t^{\mu-1}}{e^{a}a_{\varphi}}\left((a_{\phi}\psi_{\varphi}-a_{\varphi}\psi_{\phi})\begin{pmatrix}\cos\theta\\
\sin\theta\\
\end{pmatrix}+\psi_{\varphi}\begin{pmatrix}-\sin\theta\\
\cos\theta\\
\end{pmatrix}\right)

\displaystyle{=}2t^{\mu-1}\sqrt{-\frac{\mu}{\psi_{\beta}}}\frac{\psi_{\beta}}{\psi_{\beta\varphi}}\left(\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{\psi_{\beta\phi}\psi_{\varphi}-\psi_{\beta\varphi}\psi_{\phi}}{\psi_{\beta}}\right)\begin{pmatrix}\cos\theta\\
\sin\theta\\
\end{pmatrix}+\psi_{\varphi}\begin{pmatrix}-\sin\theta\\
\cos\theta\\
\end{pmatrix}\right)

\displaystyle{=}2\left(\frac{\beta}{t}\right)^{1-\mu}\sqrt{-\frac{\mu}{\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}}}\frac{\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}}{(\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}+1)\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}}\left(\frac{\partial_{\phi}\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}\cdot\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}\overline{\psi}-(\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}+1)\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}\cdot\partial_{\phi}\overline{\psi}}{2\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}}\begin{pmatrix}\cos\theta\\
\sin\theta\\
\end{pmatrix}+\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}\overline{\psi}\begin{pmatrix}-\sin\theta\\
\cos\theta\\
\end{pmatrix}\right)

\displaystyle{=}\left|x\right|^{1-\frac{1}{\mu}}\left(-\frac{\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}(\beta,\phi)}{\mu}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\mu}-1}\frac{2\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}(\beta,\phi)}{(\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}+1)\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}(\beta,\phi)}

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\left(\frac{\partial_{\phi}\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}(\beta,\phi)\cdot\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}\overline{\psi}(\beta,\phi)-(\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}+1)\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}(\beta,\phi)\cdot\partial_{\phi}\overline{\psi}(\beta,\phi)}{2\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}(\beta,\phi)}\begin{pmatrix}\cos\theta\\
\sin\theta\\
\end{pmatrix}+\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}\overline{\psi}(\beta,\phi)\begin{pmatrix}-\sin\theta\\
\cos\theta\\
\end{pmatrix}\right)

Then, the same argument for sending
t
to zero as in Proposition
4.9
gives the result.
∎

Lastly, we investigate initial data for
w(x,t)
. The situation is a little bit different from
\psi(x,t)
or
u(x,t)
, because
w(x,t)
is not continuous generally due to lacks of regularity on
\Omega(\phi)
. Therefore, we cannot guarantee local uniform convergence of
w(x,t)
as
t
goes to zero. However, we could guarantee local
L^{p}
-convergence for
w(x,t)
, actually
including the origin
. Before dealing with convergence, we show:

Proposition 4.11
.

Assume
1\leq p<2\mu
. Then,

w(\cdot,t)\in L^{p}_{\textnormal{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})

for all
t\in(0,\infty)
with its local
L^{p}
-norm independent of
t
.

Proof.

Take an annulus
\overline{B(\varepsilon,R)}
for
R,\varepsilon>0
. Then,

\int_{\overline{B(\varepsilon,R)}}\left|w(x,t)\right|^{p}\,dx\underset{\eqref{tilde func}}{=}\int_{\overline{B(\varepsilon,R)}}\frac{1}{t^{p}}\left|\widetilde{w}(xt^{-\mu})\right|^{p}\,dx\underset{xt^{-\mu}\rightarrow z}{=}\int_{\overline{B(\varepsilon t^{-\mu},Rt^{-\mu})}}\frac{1}{t^{p}}\left|\widetilde{w}(z)\right|^{p}t^{2\mu}\,dz.

By (
4.15
) and (
4.1
), we have

\left|x\right|^{-\frac{1}{\mu}}\left(\frac{1}{2\mu}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\mu}}t\leq\beta\leq\left|x\right|^{-\frac{1}{\mu}}\left(\frac{3}{2\mu}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\mu}}t.

Therefore,
T^{-1}\left(\overline{B(\varepsilon t^{-\mu},Rt^{-\mu})}\right)
, where
T
is the coordinate change map in Proposition
4.1
, is contained in

\left[R^{-\frac{1}{\mu}}\left(\frac{1}{2\mu}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\mu}}t,~~\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{\mu}}\left(\frac{3}{2\mu}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\mu}}t\right]\times\mathbb{T}.

Hence,

\displaystyle\int_{\overline{B(\varepsilon t^{-\mu},Rt^{-\mu})}}\frac{1}{t^{p}}\left|\widetilde{w}(z)\right|^{p}t^{2\mu}\,dz

(4.19)

\displaystyle\underset{(z_{1},z_{2})\xrightarrow{T^{-1}}(\beta,\phi),\,\eqref{determinant}}{}

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\leq}t^{2\mu-p}\int_{R^{-\frac{1}{\mu}}\left(\frac{1}{2\mu}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\mu}}t}^{\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{\mu}}\left(\frac{3}{2\mu}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\mu}}t}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\left|w(\beta,\phi)\right|^{p}e^{2a}a_{\varphi}\,d\beta d\phi

\displaystyle\underset{\eqref{new coordinate},\eqref{w formula}}{}

\displaystyle{\displaystyle=}\int_{R^{-\frac{1}{\mu}}\left(\frac{1}{2\mu}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\mu}}t}^{\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{\mu}}\left(\frac{3}{2\mu}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\mu}}t}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\left(-\frac{\psi_{\beta\varphi}}{\mu}\right)(\psi_{\varphi})^{-\frac{p}{2\mu}}\left|\Omega\right|^{p}\,d\beta d\phi

\displaystyle\underset{\eqref{varphi and bar var phi},\eqref{betavarphi and bar betavarphi}}{}

\displaystyle{\displaystyle=}\int_{R^{-\frac{1}{\mu}}\left(\frac{1}{2\mu}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\mu}}t}^{\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{\mu}}\left(\frac{3}{2\mu}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\mu}}t}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\beta^{p-2\mu-1}\frac{(\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}+1)\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}}{-\mu}(\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}\overline{\psi})^{-\frac{p}{2\mu}}\left|\Omega\right|^{p}\,d\beta d\phi

\displaystyle\underset{\eqref{boundedness of bar derivative},\text{Fubini}}{}

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\leq}3\cdot 2^{\frac{p}{2\mu}}t^{2\mu-p}\cdot\int_{\mathbb{T}}\left|\Omega\right|^{p}\,d\phi\cdot\int_{[R^{-\frac{1}{\mu}}\left(\frac{1}{2\mu}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\mu}}t,~~\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{\mu}}\left(\frac{3}{2\mu}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\mu}}t]}\beta^{p-2\mu-1}\,d\beta

\displaystyle=\frac{3\cdot 2^{\frac{p}{2\mu}}}{2\mu-p}\cdot\int_{\mathbb{T}}\left|\Omega\right|^{p}\,d\phi\cdot\left(\left(\frac{1}{2\mu}\right)^{\frac{p}{2\mu}-1}R^{2-\frac{p}{\mu}}-\left(\frac{3}{2\mu}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\mu}}\varepsilon^{2-\frac{p}{\mu}}\right).

Taking
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
in (
4.19
), we have

w(\cdot,t)\in L^{p}(\overline{B(0,R)})

with its time-independent norm bound

\left\|w(\cdot,t)\right\|_{L^{p}(\overline{B(0,R)})}\leq\left(\frac{6\mu}{2\mu-p}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}\mu^{-\frac{1}{2\mu}}\left\|\Omega\right\|)_{L^{p}(\mathbb{T})}R^{\frac{2}{p}-\frac{1}{\mu}}.

(4.20)

∎

Now, we state convergence of
w
as
t\rightarrow 0
in
L^{p}_{\textnormal{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
.

Proposition 4.12
.

For
1\leq p<2\mu
, the vorticity
w(x,t)
satisfies

\lim_{t\searrow 0}w(x,t)=\left|x\right|^{-\frac{1}{\mu}}\left(\frac{\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}(0,\theta)}{-\mu\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}\overline{\psi}(0,\theta)}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\mu}}\Omega(\theta),

(4.21)

where the convergence is local
L^{p}
-convergence on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
.

Proof.

We first anticipate
\lim_{t\rightarrow 0}w(x,t)
. From the equality

\displaystyle w(x,t)\underset{\eqref{tilde func}}{}

\displaystyle{\displaystyle=}t^{-1}\widetilde{w}(xt^{-\mu})\underset{(\beta,\phi)=T^{-1}(xt^{-\mu})}{\overset{\eqref{w formula}}{=}}t^{-1}\left(\psi_{\varphi}(\beta,\phi)\right)^{-\frac{1}{2\mu}}\Omega(\phi)

(4.22)

\displaystyle\underset{\eqref{varphi and bar var phi}}{}

\displaystyle{\displaystyle=}\frac{\beta}{t}\left(\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}\overline{\psi}(\beta,\phi)\right)^{-\frac{1}{2\mu}}\Omega(\phi)

\displaystyle\underset{\beta+\phi=\theta,\,\eqref{eq52}}{}

\displaystyle{\displaystyle=}\left|x\right|^{-\frac{1}{\mu}}\left(-\frac{\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}(\beta,\theta-\beta)}{\mu\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}\overline{\psi}(\beta,\theta-\beta)}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\mu}}\Omega(\theta-\beta)

and the fact that
t\rightarrow 0
corresponds to
(\beta,\phi)\rightarrow(0,\theta)
on each compact set of
\mathbb{R}^{2}\backslash\{0\}
as seen in (
4.17
), we can guess
18
18

18

This is not so rigorous, because the Proposition
4.12
deal with local
L^{p}
-convergence including the origin, while (
4.17
) does not.

\lim_{t\searrow 0}w(x,t)=\left|x\right|^{-\frac{1}{\mu}}\left(\frac{\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}(0,\theta)}{-\mu\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}\overline{\psi}(0,\theta)}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\mu}}\Omega(\theta)

in
L^{p}_{\textnormal{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
. We prove this rigorously. For
R>0,t>0
,

\displaystyle\int_{\overline{B(0,R)}}\left|w(x,t)-\left|x\right|^{-\frac{1}{\mu}}\left(\frac{\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}(0,\theta)}{-\mu\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}\overline{\psi}(0,\theta)}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\mu}}\Omega(\theta)\right|^{p}\,dx

(4.23)

\displaystyle\underset{\eqref{tilde func}}{}

\displaystyle{\displaystyle=}\int_{\overline{B(0,R)}}\left|\frac{1}{t}\widetilde{w}(xt^{-\mu})-\left|x\right|^{-\frac{1}{\mu}}\left(\frac{\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}(0,\theta)}{-\mu\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}\overline{\psi}(0,\theta)}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\mu}}\Omega(\theta)\right|^{p}\,dx

\displaystyle\underset{xt^{-\mu}\rightarrow z}{}

\displaystyle{\displaystyle=}\int_{\overline{B(0,Rt^{-\mu})}}\left|\frac{1}{t}\widetilde{w}(z)-\frac{1}{t}\left|z\right|^{-\frac{1}{\mu}}\left(\frac{\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}(0,\theta)}{-\mu\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}\overline{\psi}(0,\theta)}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\mu}}\Omega(\theta)\right|^{p}t^{2\mu}\,dz

\displaystyle\underset{(z_{1},z_{2})\rightarrow(\beta,\phi)}{}

\displaystyle{\displaystyle=}\int\int_{T^{-1}(\overline{B(0,Rt^{-\mu})})}t^{2\mu-p}\left|w(\beta,\phi)-e^{-\frac{a(\beta,\phi)}{\mu}}\left(\frac{\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}(0,\beta+\phi)}{-\mu\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}\overline{\psi}(0,\beta+\phi)}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\mu}}\Omega(\beta+\phi)\right|^{p}e^{2a}a_{\varphi}\,d\beta d\phi

\displaystyle\underset{\eqref{w formula},\eqref{new coordinate}}{}

\displaystyle{\displaystyle=}\int\int_{T^{-1}(\overline{B(0,Rt^{-\mu})})}t^{2\mu-p}\left|\left(\psi_{\varphi}(\beta,\phi)\right)^{-\frac{1}{2\mu}}\Omega(\phi)-\left(-\frac{\psi_{\beta}(\beta,\phi)}{\mu}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2\mu}}\left(\frac{\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}(0,\beta+\phi)}{-\mu\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}\overline{\psi}(0,\beta+\phi)}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\mu}}\Omega(\beta+\phi)\right|^{p}

\displaystyle\cdot\left(-\frac{\psi_{\beta\varphi}}{\mu}\right)\,d\beta d\phi

\displaystyle\underset{\eqref{beta and bar beta},\eqref{varphi and bar var phi},\eqref{betavarphi and bar betavarphi}}{}

\displaystyle{\displaystyle=}\int\int_{T^{-1}(\overline{B(0,Rt^{-\mu})})}t^{2\mu-p}\beta^{p-2\mu-1}\cdot\left(-\frac{(\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}+1)\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}(\beta,\phi)}{\mu}\right)

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\Omega(\phi)}{(\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}\overline{\psi}(\beta,\phi))^{\frac{1}{2\mu}}}-\frac{1}{(\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}\overline{\psi}(0,\beta+\phi))^{\frac{1}{2\mu}}}\left(\frac{-\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}(0,\beta+\phi)}{-\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}(\beta,\phi)}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\mu}}\Omega(\beta+\phi)\right|^{p}\,d\beta d\phi

\displaystyle\underset{\eqref{boundedness of three functions}}{}

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\leq}3\int\int_{T^{-1}(\overline{B(0,Rt^{-\mu})})}t^{2\mu-p}\beta^{p-2\mu-1}

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\Omega(\phi)}{(\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}\overline{\psi}(\beta,\phi))^{\frac{1}{2\mu}}}-\frac{1}{(\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}\overline{\psi}(0,\beta+\phi))^{\frac{1}{2\mu}}}\left(\frac{-\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}(0,\beta+\phi)}{-\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}(\beta,\phi)}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\mu}}\Omega(\beta+\phi)\right|^{p}\,d\beta d\phi

\displaystyle\underset{\beta\rightarrow t\gamma,\,\eqref{eq52},\eqref{boundedness of bar derivative}}{}

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\leq}3\int_{\mathbb{T}}\int_{(2\mu)^{-\frac{1}{2\mu}}R^{-\frac{1}{\mu}}}^{\infty}\gamma^{p-2\mu-1}

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\Omega(\phi)}{(\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}\overline{\psi}(t\gamma,\phi))^{\frac{1}{2\mu}}}-\frac{1}{(\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}\overline{\psi}(0,t\gamma+\phi))^{\frac{1}{2\mu}}}\left(\frac{-\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}(0,t\gamma+\phi)}{-\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}(t\gamma,\phi)}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\mu}}\Omega(t\gamma+\phi)\right|^{p}\,d\gamma d\phi

\displaystyle\leq 3\cdot 2^{p}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\int_{(2\mu)^{-\frac{1}{2\mu}}R^{-\frac{1}{\mu}}}^{\infty}\gamma^{p-2\mu-1}\frac{1}{(\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}\overline{\psi}(t\gamma,\phi))^{\frac{p}{2\mu}}}\left|\Omega(\phi)-\Omega(t\gamma+\phi)\right|^{p}\,d\gamma d\phi

\displaystyle+3\cdot 2^{p}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\int_{(2\mu)^{-\frac{1}{2\mu}}R^{-\frac{1}{\mu}}}^{\infty}\gamma^{p-2\mu-1}\Bigg\lvert\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}\overline{\psi}(t\gamma,\phi)}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\mu}}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{(\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}\overline{\psi}(0,t\gamma+\phi))^{\frac{1}{2\mu}}}\left(\frac{-\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}(0,t\gamma+\phi)}{-\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}(t\gamma,\phi)}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\mu}}\Bigg\rvert^{p}\left|\Omega(t\gamma+\phi)\right|^{p}\,d\gamma d\phi

\displaystyle\underset{\eqref{boundedness of bar derivative},\,\text{Fubini}}{}

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\leq}3\cdot 2^{p+\frac{p}{2\mu}}\int_{(2\mu)^{-\frac{1}{2\mu}}R^{-\frac{1}{\mu}}}^{\infty}\gamma^{p-2\mu-1}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\left|\Omega(\phi)-\Omega(t\gamma+\phi)\right|^{p}\,d\phi d\gamma

\displaystyle+3\cdot 2^{p}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\int_{(2\mu)^{-\frac{1}{2\mu}}R^{-\frac{1}{\mu}}}^{\infty}\gamma^{p-2\mu-1}\Bigg\lvert\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}\overline{\psi}(t\gamma,\phi)}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\mu}}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{(\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}\overline{\psi}(0,t\gamma+\phi))^{\frac{1}{2\mu}}}\left(\frac{-\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}(0,t\gamma+\phi)}{-\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}(t\gamma,\phi)}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\mu}}\Bigg\rvert^{p}\left|\Omega(t\gamma+\phi)\right|^{p}\,d\gamma d\phi.

Since

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\int_{\mathbb{T}}\left|\Omega(\phi)-\Omega(t\gamma+\phi)\right|^{p}\xrightarrow[t\searrow 0]{}0\qquad\text{ for all }\gamma>0,\\
&\Bigg\lvert\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}\overline{\psi}(t\gamma,\phi)}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\mu}}-\frac{1}{(\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}\overline{\psi}(0,t\gamma+\phi))^{\frac{1}{2\mu}}}\left(\frac{-\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}(0,t\gamma+\phi)}{-\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}(t\gamma,\phi)}\right)^{\frac{1}{2\mu}}\Bigg\rvert^{p}\xrightarrow[t\searrow 0]{}0\qquad\text{ for all }\gamma>0,\phi\in\mathbb{T},\\
&\int_{\mathbb{T}}\int_{(2\mu)^{-\frac{1}{2\mu}}}^{\infty}\gamma^{p-2\mu-1}\left|\Omega(\phi)\right|^{p}\,d\gamma d\phi\qquad\text{ is integrable},\end{aligned}\right.

Lebesgue’s dominate convergence theorem yields

\lim_{t\searrow 0}\left\|w(\cdot,t)-w(\cdot,0)\right\|_{L^{p}(\overline{B(0,R)})}=0.

∎

Definition 4.13
.

From now on, we define initial data
(w(\cdot,0),u(\cdot,0),\psi(\cdot,0))
by the right hand side of (
4.21
), (
4.18
), (
4.12
).

Combining results and their proof of Proposition
4.9
,
4.10
,
4.11
,
4.12
, we have the following boundedness property.

Corollary 4.14
.

There exists a positive constant
A=A(\Omega)
and
C=C(R)
such that

\left|\psi(x,t)\right|\leq A\left|x\right|^{2-\frac{1}{\mu}},\qquad\left|u(x,t)\right|\leq A\left|x\right|^{1-\frac{1}{\mu}},\qquad\left\|w(\cdot,t)\right\|_{L^{p}(\overline{B(0,R)})}\leq C

for all
1\leq p<2\mu
and
t\in[0,\infty)
.

4.4.
The spiral roll-up phenomenon

As we clarified initial vorticity in Proposition
4.10
, we can show spiral roll-up phenomenon of
w(x,t)
. Let
\phi_{0}\in\mathbb{T}
be an angle such that
\Omega(\phi_{0})=0
. Then, the line in
(\beta,\phi)
- coordinate

\ell\coloneqq\left\{(\beta,\phi_{0})\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{T}\,|\,\beta\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\right\}

is transformed to

T(\ell)=\left(-\frac{\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}(\beta,\phi_{0}))}{\mu}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\beta^{-\mu}\Big(\cos(\beta+\phi_{0}),\sin(\beta+\phi_{0})\Big),\qquad\beta\in\mathbb{R}_{+}

(4.24)

in
(z_{1},z_{2})
- coordinate by (
2.12
), (
2.13
) and (
2.35
). Since

\sqrt{\frac{1}{2\mu}}\leq\left(-\frac{\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}(\beta,\phi_{0})}{\mu}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\sqrt{\frac{3}{2\mu}}

for all
(\beta,\phi)\in\overline{\mathbb{R}}_{+}\times\mathbb{T}
by (
4.1
),
T(\ell)
is nearly an algebraic spiral.

Figure 4.
Line
\ell=\{\phi=\phi_{0}\}
is push forwarded to nearly algebraic spiral, i.e.,
T(\ell)\sim\left|z\right|^{-\mu}
.

As

\widetilde{w}\left(T(\beta,\phi_{0})\right)\underset{\eqref{pull back notation}}{=}w(\beta,\phi_{0})\underset{\eqref{w formula}}{=}\beta\left(\overline{\partial}_{\varphi}\overline{\psi}(\beta,\phi_{0})\right)^{-\frac{1}{2\mu}}\Omega(\phi_{0})=0

(4.25)

for all
\beta\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
, we have
\widetilde{w}(z)=0
for all
z\in T(\ell)\subset\mathbb{R}^{2}\backslash\left\{0\right\}
. Since

w(x,t)=\frac{1}{t}\widetilde{w}(xt^{-\mu})

we get that

w(x,t)=0

for all

x\in t^{\mu}T(\ell)\underset{\eqref{eq91}}{=}\left(-\frac{\overline{\partial}_{\beta}\overline{\psi}(\beta,\phi_{0}))}{\mu}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\underbrace{\left(\frac{t}{\beta}\right)^{\mu}\Big(\cos(\beta+\phi_{0}),\sin(\beta+\phi_{0})\Big)}_{\text{algebraic spiral for all }\phi_{0}\in\mathbb{T},\,t>0}\subset\mathbb{R}^{2}\backslash\left\{0\right\}.

Also, (
4.25
) implies that
\widetilde{w}(T(\beta,\phi_{1}))\neq 0
for
\phi_{1}
such that
\Omega(\phi_{1})\neq 0
. This shows spiral roll-up phenomenon in Definition
1.7
. Note that as
t\rightarrow 0
,
t^{\mu}T(\ell)
converges to half-line with angle
\theta=\phi_{0}
as shown in Proposition
4.12
.

Figure 5.
Spiral roll-up phenomenon.

4.5.
Weak solution on
\mathbb{R}^{2}\times[0,\infty)

Having clarified initial data as in Definition
4.13
and investigated time independent boundedness in Corollary
4.14
, we can prove that
\psi,u,w
is a weak solution of Euler equation including time zero and origin. First, we treat velocity form. The proof is almost same as in [Elling
[
12
]
, Section 8.3.].

Proposition 4.15
.

u_{t}+\nabla_{x}\cdot(u\otimes u)+\nabla_{x}p=0

on
\mathbb{R}^{2}\times[0,\infty)
in the sense of distribution, i.e., for any divergence free vector field
g\in C^{\infty}_{c}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}\times[0,\infty);\mathbb{R}^{2}\right)
,

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}u(x,0)\cdot g(x,0)\,dx+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}\times[0,\infty)}u\cdot g_{t}+(u\otimes u):\nabla_{x}g\,dxdt=0.

Proof.

As we already proved Proposition
4.8
, what we have to consider is integration near the origin and time zero. Consider a smooth, radial cut-off function
\zeta:\mathbb{R}^{2}\rightarrow[0,1]
such that

\zeta(r)\equiv 1\quad\text{on}\quad B(0,1),\qquad\zeta(r)\equiv 0\quad\text{on}\quad\mathbb{R}^{2}\backslash B(0,2)

and define
\zeta^{\delta}
by
\zeta\left(\frac{\cdot}{\delta}\right)
. Then,

\mathrm{supp}(\zeta^{\delta})\subset\overline{B(0,2\delta)},\qquad\mathrm{supp}(\nabla_{x}\zeta^{\delta})\subset\overline{B(0,2\delta)}\backslash B(0,\delta),\qquad\mathrm{supp}(1-\zeta^{\delta})\subset\mathbb{R}^{2}\backslash B(0,\delta)

(4.26)

and there exists
M_{1}>0
such that

\left\|\nabla_{x}\zeta^{\delta}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}<\frac{M_{1}}{\delta},\qquad\left\|\nabla_{x}^{2}\zeta^{\delta}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}<\frac{M_{1}}{\delta^{2}}.

(4.27)

Now, we analyze a test function. Since
g
is a divergence free test function, we can take a scalar function
f\in C^{\infty}_{c}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}\times[0,\infty)\right)
such that
\nabla_{x}^{\perp}f=g
. Replacing
f(x,t)
with
f(x,t)-f(0,t)
, we can assume that
f(0,t)=0
for all
t\in[0,\infty)
.
19
19

19

After replacing,
f
has not compact support anymore, but it does not matter since we will only treat
f
after cut-off or differentiation.
As
f_{t}(0,t)=0
for all
t\in[0,\infty)
also, there exists
M_{2}>0
and
R>0
such that

\left|f(x,t)\right|,\left|f_{t}(x,t)\right|\leq M_{2}\left|x\right|

(4.28)

for all
0\leq\left|x\right|\leq R
and
t\geq 0
, and

\left\|\nabla f(\cdot,t)\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})},\left\|\nabla^{2}f(\cdot,t)\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})},\left\|\nabla f_{t}(\cdot,t)\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})},\left\|\nabla^{\perp}f_{t}(\cdot,t)\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq M_{2}

(4.29)

for all
t\geq 0
. Let
M=\textnormal{max}\left\{M_{1},M_{2}\right\}
. Then, for all
0<\delta\leq\text{min}\left\{\frac{R}{2},\frac{1}{M}\right\}
and
t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla(\zeta^{\delta}f(\cdot,t))\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

(4.30)

\displaystyle\underset{\eqref{eq66}}{}

\displaystyle{\displaystyle=}\left\|\nabla(\zeta^{\delta}f(\cdot,t))\right\|_{L^{\infty}(B(0,2\delta))}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\nabla\zeta^{\delta}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(B(0,2\delta))}\left\|f(\cdot,t)\right\|_{L^{\infty}(B(0,2\delta))}+\left\|\nabla f(\cdot,t)\right\|_{L^{\infty}(B(0,2\delta))}\left\|\zeta^{\delta}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(B(0,2\delta))}

\displaystyle\underset{\eqref{eq67},\eqref{eq68},\eqref{eq69}}{}

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\leq}\frac{M}{\delta}\cdot 2M\delta+M\cdot 1

\displaystyle\leq 2M^{2}+1,

and
\left\|\nabla(\zeta^{\delta}f_{t}(\cdot,t))\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}
has same bound. Similarly, for all
t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla^{2}(\zeta^{\delta}f(\cdot,t))\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}

(4.31)

\displaystyle\underset{\eqref{eq66}}{}

\displaystyle{\displaystyle=}\left\|\nabla^{2}(\zeta^{\delta}f(\cdot,t))\right\|_{L^{\infty}(B(0,2\delta))}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\nabla^{2}\zeta^{\delta}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(B(0,2\delta))}\left\|f(\cdot,t)\right\|_{L^{\infty}(B(0,2\delta))}+2\left\|\nabla\zeta^{\delta}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(B(0,2\delta))}\left\|\nabla f(\cdot,t)\right\|_{L^{\infty}(B(0,2\delta))}

\displaystyle+\left\|\zeta^{\delta}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(B(0,2\delta))}\left\|\nabla^{2}f(\cdot,t)\right\|_{L^{\infty}(B(0,2\delta))}

\displaystyle\underset{\eqref{eq67},\eqref{eq68},\eqref{eq69}}{}

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\leq}\frac{M}{\delta^{2}}\cdot 2M\delta+\frac{2M}{\delta}\cdot M+1\cdot M

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4M^{2}+1}{\delta}.

Now, it is time to use
\mu>\frac{2}{3}
condition crucially. Let

p\coloneqq\begin{cases}1+\frac{1}{2\left(\frac{1}{\mu}-1\right)}=\frac{2-\mu}{2(1-\mu)}&(\frac{2}{3}<\mu<1)\\
